[57] [assistant_text] All five files are created. Here's what each does:

**[Makefile](Makefile)** — standard `make` / `make debug` / `make release` / `make clean`. Picks up all `*.cpp` files under `src/` recursively, mirrors the directory structure into `build/`.

**[build.bat](build.bat)** / **[build.sh](build.sh)** — takes an optional `debug` argument (default: `release`). Tries `clang++` first; if it exits non-zero, automatically retries with `clang-cl` using the equivalent MSVC-style flags (`/std:c++23`, `/O2`, etc.).

**[run.bat](run.bat)** / **[run.sh](run.sh)** — checks for the binary, builds if missing, then executes it. Any arguments are forwarded to the binary.

Next step is creating `src/main.cpp` to give the build system something to compile.

I found comprehensive information about NASA and JPL's C coding standards. Here's what I discovered:

## Overview

The Power of 10 Rules were created in 2006 by Gerard J. Holzmann of the NASA/JPL Laboratory for Reliable Software. The rules are intended to eliminate certain C coding practices that make code difficult to review or statically analyze. These rules are a complement to the MISRA C guidelines and have been incorporated into the greater set of JPL coding standards.

## Key Rules

The 10 main rules include:

1. Restrict all code to very simple control flow constructs—do not use goto statements, setjmp or longjmp constructs, or direct or indirect recursion.

2. Give all loops a fixed upper bound—it must be trivially possible for a checking tool to prove statically that the loop cannot exceed a preset upper bound on the number of iterations.

3. Do not use dynamic memory allocation after initialization.

4. No function should be longer than what can be printed on a single sheet of paper in a standard format with one line per statement and one line per declaration.

5. Each calling function must check the return value of nonvoid functions, and each called function must check the validity of all parameters provided by the caller.

6. The use of the preprocessor must be limited to the inclusion of header files and simple macros definitions—token pasting, variable argument lists (ellipses), and recursive macro calls are not allowed.

7. The use of pointers must be restricted—specifically, no more than one level of dereference should be used.

8. All code must be compiled, from the first day of development, with all compiler warnings enabled at the most pedantic setting available.

## Formal Standard

The relatively effortless adoption of the Power of Ten rules led to the creation of a new formal "JPL Institutional Coding Standard" that combines the Power of Ten rules with a small number of additional rules that are more specific to the spacecraft context.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about JPL C coding standards:

## Overview
The JPL Coding Standard for C programming language defines rules across several levels of compliance that primarily target the development of mission critical flight software. The JPL code standards are based on the 2004 MISRA-C coding standard.

## Key Areas Covered

**Static Analysis and Compilation:**
Among the requirements for C-language code at JPL: Compile with all warnings enabled; use static source code analyzers.

**Function Length:**
Use short functions with a limited number of parameters. Additionally, the ability to customize the rules allows for quick experimentation, such as by defining the limit on function length, or the minimal number of assertions per functions of a given minimal size.

**General Rules:**
The rules focus on aspects like language compliance, predictable execution, defensive coding and code clarity, addressing issues like loop bounds, recursion, memory protection, assertions and limiting preprocessor usage.

**Preprocessor Usage:**
In some cases, the JPL Coding Standard puts additional restriction on what features of C can be used—preprocessor features are especially constrained.

However, the search results do not contain specific information about header guard naming conventions in the JPL C coding standard. You may need to consult the official JPL Institutional Coding Standard document directly for those specific details.


**Key design decisions following JPL rules:**

| Rule | How it's applied |
|------|-----------------|
| No recursion | `var_walk` is an explicit `for` loop; `probe_slot` is a linear scan |
| Fixed loop bounds | Every loop caps at `ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY` or `VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY` |
| No post-init malloc | All storage is pre-declared inside `AtomTable` / `VarRegistry` |
| Pointer depth ≤ 1 | `entry_at()` returns a single-level `VarEntry *`; no `**` anywhere |
| Parameter validation | Every public function returns `-1` / `ATOM_ID_INVALID` on bad input |
| Assertions | `assert()` guards internal invariants (full table before insert, etc.) |
| `strncpy` avoided | Used `memcpy` + explicit NUL to dodge the MSVC deprecation warning |

174	
175	## Phase 0.6: Predictive Execution
176	
177	Precompute likely future queries on idle cores.
178	
179	### Goals
180	- Learn query patterns from statistics (0.2)
181	- Speculatively compute and cache results before they are requested
182	
183	### Tasks
184	
185	- [ ] Pattern learner
186	  - Identify query sequences that co-occur frequently
187	  - Build a Markov chain or simple frequency table
188	
189	- [ ] Speculative executor
190	  - On query A completion, schedule likely query B on an idle core
191	  - Store result in speculative cache with TTL
192	
193	- [ ] Cache management
194	  - On actual query B arrival: serve from cache, record hit
195	  - On TTL expiry or cache pressure: discard silently
196	  - Never serve stale speculative results for non-pure predicates
197	
198	### Invariant
199	Speculative results must never be returned if the fact base changed between speculation and query arrival.
200	
201	---

206

210	- Parallel rule evaluation for independent subgoals

212	### Tasks

214	- [ ] Work-stealing scheduler
215	  - Split choice points across worker threads
216	  - Merge result streams in program order
217	
218	- [ ] Parallel conjunction
219	  - Detect independent body goals (no shared variables)
220	  - Execute them concurrently, join results
221	
222	- [ ] Concurrency safety
223	  - Immutable substitution environments (share-nothing)
224	  - Lock-free fact store reads; serialized writes

243	
244	These apply across all phases:
245	
246	- **Correctness over speed**: never let an optimization change a logical result
247	- **Layered architecture**: inference core has no dependency on prediction or cache layers
248	- **Testability**: each layer must be testable in isolation

[136] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * atoms.c — Atom table implementation for the Epilog inference engine.
3	 *
4	 * Strategy: open-addressed hash table with linear probing.
5	 * All storage is pre-allocated in AtomTable (no malloc after init).
6	 *
7	 * JPL rules observed:
8	 *   Rule 1  — no goto, no setjmp/longjmp, no recursion.
9	 *   Rule 2  — every loop has a fixed upper bound (ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY).
10	 *   Rule 3  — no dynamic memory allocation after atom_table_init().
11	 *   Rule 4  — no function exceeds ~60 lines.
12	 *   Rule 5  — every parameter validated; every return value is meaningful.
13	 *   Rule 6  — preprocessor used only for include and simple constants.
14	 *   Rule 7  — pointer depth ≤ 1 in all operations.
15	 *   Rule 8  — compiled with -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic.
16	 */
17	
18	#include "../include/atoms.h"
19	
20	#include <assert.h>   /* assert()   */
21	#include <string.h>   /* memset, strncpy, strlen, strncmp */
22	#include <stdint.h>
23	
24	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
25	 * Internal helpers
26	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
27	
28	/* fnv1a_32 — FNV-1a 32-bit hash over a NUL-terminated string. */
29	static uint32_t fnv1a_32(const char *s)
30	{
31	    uint32_t hash = 2166136261U; /* FNV offset basis */
32	    uint32_t i;
33	
34	    assert(s != NULL);
35	
36	    for (i = 0U; i < ATOM_MAX_LEN + 1U && s[i] != '\0'; ++i) {
37	        hash ^= (uint32_t)(unsigned char)s[i];
38	        hash *= 16777619U; /* FNV prime */
39	    }
40	    return hash;
41	}
42	
43	/*
44	 * probe_slot — linear-probe search.
45	 * Returns the index of the matching slot or the first empty slot.
46	 * *found is set to 1 if the atom already exists, 0 otherwise.
47	 * Returns ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY when the table is full with no match.
48	 */
49	static uint32_t probe_slot(const AtomTable *t, const char *spelling,
50	                            uint32_t hash, int *found)
51	{
52	    uint32_t idx  = hash % ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY;
53	    uint32_t step = 0U;
54	
55	    assert(t       != NULL);
56	    assert(spelling != NULL);
57	    assert(found   != NULL);
58	
59	    *found = 0;
60	
61	    for (step = 0U; step < ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY; ++step) {
62	        const Atom *slot = &t->slots[idx];
63	
64	        if (slot->id == ATOM_ID_INVALID) {
65	            return idx; /* empty — can insert here */
66	        }
67	
68	        if (slot->hash == hash &&
69	            strncmp(slot->spelling, spelling, ATOM_MAX_LEN) == 0) {
70	            *found = 1;
71	            return idx; /* existing match */
72	        }
73	
74	        idx = (idx + 1U) % ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY;
75	    }
76	
77	    return ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY; /* table full, no match */
78	}
79	
80	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
81	 * Public API
82	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
83	
84	int atom_table_init(AtomTable *t)
85	{
86	    if (t == NULL) {
87	        return -1;
88	    }
89	    memset(t, 0, sizeof(AtomTable));
90	    return 0;
91	}
92	
93	AtomId atom_intern(AtomTable *t, const char *spelling)
94	{
95	    uint32_t hash;
96	    uint32_t idx;
97	    int      found;
98	    size_t   len;
99	    Atom    *slot;
100	
101	    if (t == NULL || spelling == NULL) {
102	        return ATOM_ID_INVALID;
103	    }
104	
105	    len = strnlen(spelling, ATOM_MAX_LEN + 1U);
106	    if (len > ATOM_MAX_LEN) {
107	        return ATOM_ID_INVALID;
108	    }
109	
110	    hash = fnv1a_32(spelling);
111	    idx  = probe_slot(t, spelling, hash, &found);
112	
113	    if (idx == ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY) {
114	        return ATOM_ID_INVALID; /* table full */
115	    }
116	
117	    if (found) {
118	        return t->slots[idx].id;
119	    }
120	
121	    /* Insert new atom. */
122	    assert(t->count < ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY);
123	
124	    slot = &t->slots[idx];
125	    /* memcpy + explicit NUL — avoids MSVC strncpy deprecation warning. */
126	    memcpy(slot->spelling, spelling, len);
127	    slot->spelling[len] = '\0';
128	    slot->hash = hash;
129	    slot->id   = (AtomId)(t->count + ATOM_ID_MIN);
130	
131	    t->count++;
132	    return slot->id;
133	}
134	
135	AtomId atom_lookup(const AtomTable *t, const char *spelling)
136	{
137	    uint32_t hash;
138	    uint32_t idx;
139	    int      found;
140	    size_t   len;
141	
142	    if (t == NULL || spelling == NULL) {
143	        return ATOM_ID_INVALID;
144	    }
145	
146	    len = strnlen(spelling, ATOM_MAX_LEN + 1U);
147	    if (len > ATOM_MAX_LEN) {
148	        return ATOM_ID_INVALID;
149	    }
150	
151	    hash = fnv1a_32(spelling);
152	    idx  = probe_slot(t, spelling, hash, &found);
153	
154	    if (!found || idx == ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY) {
155	        return ATOM_ID_INVALID;
156	    }
157	
158	    return t->slots[idx].id;
159	}
160	
161	const char *atom_spelling(const AtomTable *t, AtomId id)
162	{
163	    uint32_t slot_index;
164	    uint32_t i;
165	
166	    if (t == NULL || id == ATOM_ID_INVALID) {
167	        return NULL;
168	    }
169	
170	    /*
171	     * Linear scan to find the slot whose id matches.
172	     * This path is not performance-critical (used for display / debug).
173	     * Bounded by ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY (JPL Rule 2).
174	     */
175	    for (i = 0U; i < ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY; ++i) {
176	        if (t->slots[i].id == id) {
177	            return t->slots[i].spelling;
178	        }
179	    }
180	
181	    (void)slot_index; /* suppressed: unused after refactor */
182	    return NULL;
183	}
184	
185	uint32_t atom_count(const AtomTable *t)
186	{
187	    if (t == NULL) {
188	        return 0U;
189	    }
190	    return t->count;
191	}

2	 * atoms.h — Atom table for the Epilog inference engine.
3	 *
4	 * An atom is an immutable, interned string. Every unique spelling maps to
5	 * exactly one AtomId. Comparisons are therefore O(1) integer comparisons.
6	 *
7	 * JPL rules observed:
8	 *   - No dynamic allocation after atom_table_init().
9	 *   - All loops have a statically provable upper bound (ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY).
10	 *   - No recursion.
11	 *   - No more than one level of pointer indirection in public API.
12	 *   - Every function documents its preconditions; callers must check returns.
13	 */
14	
15	#ifndef EPILOG_ATOMS_H
16	#define EPILOG_ATOMS_H
17	
18	#include <stddef.h>   /* size_t  */
19	#include <stdint.h>   /* uint32_t */
20	
21	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
22	 * Compile-time limits (Rule 2: fixed upper bounds for all loops/tables).
23	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
24	
25	#define ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY  4096U   /* max distinct atoms     */
26	#define ATOM_MAX_LEN          255U   /* max chars, excl. NUL   */
27	
28	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
29	 * Public types
30	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
31	
32	/*
33	 * AtomId — opaque handle; ATOM_ID_INVALID signals absence / error.
34	 * Zero is intentionally reserved so zero-initialised structs are safe.
35	 */
36	typedef uint32_t AtomId;
37	#define ATOM_ID_INVALID  0U
38	#define ATOM_ID_MIN      1U
39	
40	/* Internal storage for one interned atom. */
41	typedef struct {
42	    char     spelling[ATOM_MAX_LEN + 1U]; /* NUL-terminated              */
43	    uint32_t hash;                         /* pre-computed FNV-1a hash    */
44	    AtomId   id;                           /* == slot index + ATOM_ID_MIN */
45	} Atom;
46	
47	/*

49	 * Allocate on the stack or as a module-level static; do NOT heap-alloc.
50	 */

52	    Atom     slots[ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY];
53	    uint32_t count;   /* number of interned atoms */
54	} AtomTable;
55

62	 * Must be called exactly once before any other atom_* function.
63	 * Returns 0 on success, -1 if t is NULL.
64	 */
65	int atom_table_init(AtomTable *t);
66

69	 * If an atom with this spelling already exists, returns its id.
70	 * If the table is full or the spelling is too long, returns ATOM_ID_INVALID.
71	 * spelling must not be NULL.
72	 */
73	AtomId atom_intern(AtomTable *t, const char *spelling);

85	 */
86	const char *atom_spelling(const AtomTable *t, AtomId id);
87

[140] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * variables.h — Logic variable registry for the Epilog inference engine.
3	 *
4	 * A logic variable is an unbound or bound slot in a unification frame.
5	 * Variables are identified by a VarId scoped to a VarRegistry that lives
6	 * for the duration of a single query. The registry is reset between queries;
7	 * no heap allocation occurs after init.
8	 *
9	 * Binding is represented as a union-find structure without path compression
10	 * to keep pointer depth ≤ 1 and behaviour fully predictable (JPL rule 7).
11	 * Binding chains are therefore at most VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY long, which is
12	 * statically bounded (JPL rule 2).
13	 *
14	 * JPL rules observed:
15	 *   - No dynamic allocation after var_registry_init().
16	 *   - All loops have a statically provable upper bound.
17	 *   - No recursion — walk() is an explicit bounded loop.
18	 *   - No more than one level of pointer indirection in public API.
19	 *   - Every function checks its parameters; callers must check returns.
20	 */
21	
22	#ifndef EPILOG_VARIABLES_H
23	#define EPILOG_VARIABLES_H
24	
25	#include <stddef.h>    /* size_t   */
26	#include <stdint.h>    /* uint32_t */
27	#include "atoms.h"     /* AtomId   */
28	
29	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
30	 * Compile-time limits
31	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
32	
33	#define VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY  1024U  /* max variables per query frame */
34	#define VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN         63U  /* max chars in a variable name  */
35	
36	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
37	 * Public types
38	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
39	
40	/*
41	 * VarId — opaque handle. VAR_ID_INVALID signals absence / error.
42	 */
43	typedef uint32_t VarId;
44	#define VAR_ID_INVALID  0U
45	#define VAR_ID_MIN      1U
46	
47	/*
48	 * TermTag — discriminates what a bound variable holds.
49	 */
50	typedef enum {
51	    TERM_UNBOUND = 0, /* variable is free          */
52	    TERM_ATOM,        /* bound to an interned atom  */
53	    TERM_VAR          /* bound to another variable  */
54	} TermTag;
55	
56	/*
57	 * Binding — the value slot inside a VarEntry.
58	 * Union kept flat so pointer depth never exceeds 1.
59	 */
60	typedef union {
61	    AtomId atom_id; /* valid when tag == TERM_ATOM */
62	    VarId  var_id;  /* valid when tag == TERM_VAR  */
63	} BindingValue;
64	
65	/*
66	 * VarEntry — one slot in the registry.
67	 */
68	typedef struct {
69	    char         name[VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN + 1U]; /* source-level name, may be "_" */
70	    TermTag      tag;
71	    BindingValue value;
72	    VarId        id; /* == slot index + VAR_ID_MIN */
73	} VarEntry;
74	
75	/*
76	 * VarRegistry — fixed-size store for one query's variables.
77	 * Allocate as a local or static; do NOT heap-alloc.
78	 */
79	typedef struct {
80	    VarEntry entries[VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY];
81	    uint32_t count;
82	} VarRegistry;
83	
84	/*
85	 * WalkResult — return type for var_walk.
86	 */
87	typedef struct {
88	    TermTag      tag;
89	    BindingValue value;    /* meaningful when tag != TERM_UNBOUND */
90	    VarId        root_id;  /* the canonical (deepest bound) VarId */
91	} WalkResult;
92	
93	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
94	 * Public API
95	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
96	
97	/*
98	 * var_registry_init — zero-initialise *r before first use.
99	 * Must be called once per query, before any other var_* function.
100	 * Returns 0 on success, -1 if r is NULL.
101	 */
102	int var_registry_init(VarRegistry *r);
103	
104	/*
105	 * var_alloc — allocate a fresh unbound variable named *name.
106	 * name must be NUL-terminated and ≤ VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN chars.
107	 * Returns VAR_ID_INVALID if the registry is full or name is NULL.
108	 */
109	VarId var_alloc(VarRegistry *r, const char *name);
110	
111	/*
112	 * var_bind_atom — bind variable id to atom atom_id.
113	 * Fails (returns -1) if id is already bound or either argument is invalid.
114	 * Returns 0 on success.
115	 */
116	int var_bind_atom(VarRegistry *r, VarId id, AtomId atom_id);
117	
118	/*
119	 * var_bind_var — bind variable id to another variable other_id.
120	 * Fails (returns -1) if id is already bound or either id is invalid.
121	 * Returns 0 on success.
122	 */
123	int var_bind_var(VarRegistry *r, VarId id, VarId other_id);
124	
125	/*
126	 * var_walk — follow binding chain from id to its canonical root.
127	 * Terminates in at most VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY steps (bounded loop, no
128	 * recursion). Returns a WalkResult describing the final state.
129	 * On invalid id, result.tag == TERM_UNBOUND and result.root_id == VAR_ID_INVALID.
130	 */
131	WalkResult var_walk(const VarRegistry *r, VarId id);
132	
133	/*
134	 * var_reset — clear all bindings, keeping capacity intact.
135	 * Equivalent to re-running var_registry_init on an existing registry.
136	 * Returns 0 on success, -1 if r is NULL.
137	 */
138	int var_reset(VarRegistry *r);
139	
140	/*
141	 * var_count — number of variables currently allocated in *r.
142	 */
143	uint32_t var_count(const VarRegistry *r);

145	#endif /* EPILOG_VARIABLES_H */

2	 * variables.c — Logic variable registry for the Epilog inference engine.
3	 *
4	 * Variables are allocated per-query from a fixed-size VarRegistry.
5	 * Binding chains are walked with an explicit bounded loop (no recursion).
6	 * No heap allocation occurs after var_registry_init().
7	 *
8	 * JPL rules observed:
9	 *   Rule 1  — no goto, no setjmp/longjmp, no recursion.
10	 *   Rule 2  — every loop has a fixed upper bound (VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY).
11	 *   Rule 3  — no dynamic memory allocation after var_registry_init().
12	 *   Rule 4  — no function exceeds ~60 lines.
13	 *   Rule 5  — every parameter validated; every return value is meaningful.
14	 *   Rule 6  — preprocessor used only for include and simple constants.
15	 *   Rule 7  — pointer depth ≤ 1 in all operations.
16	 *   Rule 8  — compiled with -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic.
17	 */
18	
19	#include "../include/variables.h"
20	
21	#include <assert.h>
22	#include <string.h>   /* memset, memcpy, strnlen */
23	
24	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
25	 * Internal helpers
26	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
27	
28	/*
29	 * entry_at — bounds-checked slot accessor.
30	 * Returns NULL when id is out of range; avoids pointer arithmetic elsewhere.
31	 */
32	static VarEntry *entry_at(VarRegistry *r, VarId id)
33	{
34	    uint32_t idx;
35	
36	    assert(r != NULL);
37	
38	    if (id == VAR_ID_INVALID || id < VAR_ID_MIN) {
39	        return NULL;
40	    }
41	
42	    idx = id - VAR_ID_MIN;
43	
44	    if (idx >= VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY || idx >= r->count) {
45	        return NULL;
46	    }
47	
48	    return &r->entries[idx];
49	}
50	
51	/* const variant for read-only callers */
52	static const VarEntry *entry_at_c(const VarRegistry *r, VarId id)
53	{
54	    uint32_t idx;
55	
56	    assert(r != NULL);
57	
58	    if (id == VAR_ID_INVALID || id < VAR_ID_MIN) {
59	        return NULL;
60	    }
61	
62	    idx = id - VAR_ID_MIN;
63	
64	    if (idx >= VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY || idx >= r->count) {
65	        return NULL;
66	    }
67	
68	    return &r->entries[idx];
69	}

71	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
72	 * Public API
73	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
74	
75	int var_registry_init(VarRegistry *r)
76	{
77	    if (r == NULL) {
78	        return -1;
79	    }
80	    memset(r, 0, sizeof(VarRegistry));
81	    return 0;
82	}
83	
84	VarId var_alloc(VarRegistry *r, const char *name)
85	{
86	    VarEntry *slot;
87	    size_t    len;
88	    VarId     new_id;
89	
90	    if (r == NULL || name == NULL) {
91	        return VAR_ID_INVALID;
92	    }
93	
94	    if (r->count >= VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY) {
95	        return VAR_ID_INVALID;
96	    }
97	
98	    len = strnlen(name, VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN + 1U);
99	    if (len > VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN) {
100	        return VAR_ID_INVALID;
101	    }
102	
103	    new_id = (VarId)(r->count + VAR_ID_MIN);
104	    slot   = &r->entries[r->count];
105	
106	    memset(slot, 0, sizeof(VarEntry));
107	    memcpy(slot->name, name, len);
108	    slot->name[len] = '\0';
109	    slot->tag       = TERM_UNBOUND;
110	    slot->id        = new_id;
111	
112	    r->count++;
113	    return new_id;
114	}
115	
116	int var_bind_atom(VarRegistry *r, VarId id, AtomId atom_id)
117	{
118	    VarEntry *e;
119	
120	    if (r == NULL || atom_id == ATOM_ID_INVALID) {
121	        return -1;
122	    }
123	
124	    e = entry_at(r, id);
125	    if (e == NULL) {
126	        return -1;
127	    }
128	
129	    if (e->tag != TERM_UNBOUND) {
130	        return -1; /* already bound */
131	    }
132	
133	    e->tag         = TERM_ATOM;
134	    e->value.atom_id = atom_id;
135	    return 0;
136	}
137	
138	int var_bind_var(VarRegistry *r, VarId id, VarId other_id)
139	{
140	    VarEntry *e;
141	
142	    if (r == NULL) {
143	        return -1;
144	    }
145	
146	    /* Binding a variable to itself is a no-op, not an error. */
147	    if (id == other_id) {
148	        return 0;
149	    }
150	
151	    e = entry_at(r, id);
152	    if (e == NULL) {
153	        return -1;
154	    }
155	
156	    if (entry_at(r, other_id) == NULL) {
157	        return -1; /* other_id doesn't exist */
158	    }
159	
160	    if (e->tag != TERM_UNBOUND) {
161	        return -1; /* already bound */
162	    }
163	
164	    e->tag          = TERM_VAR;
165	    e->value.var_id = other_id;
166	    return 0;
167	}
168	
169	WalkResult var_walk(const VarRegistry *r, VarId id)
170	{
171	    WalkResult     result;
172	    const VarEntry *e;
173	    VarId           current;
174	    uint32_t        steps;
175	
176	    result.tag            = TERM_UNBOUND;
177	    result.value.var_id   = VAR_ID_INVALID;
178	    result.root_id        = VAR_ID_INVALID;
179	
180	    if (r == NULL || id == VAR_ID_INVALID) {
181	        return result;
182	    }
183	
184	    current = id;
185	
186	    /*
187	     * Follow the binding chain. Bounded by VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY so the
188	     * loop is statically provable to terminate (JPL Rule 2).
189	     * No path compression — keeps pointer depth ≤ 1 (JPL Rule 7).
190	     */
191	    for (steps = 0U; steps < VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY; ++steps) {
192	        e = entry_at_c(r, current);
193	        if (e == NULL) {
194	            return result; /* dangling id — return invalid */
195	        }
196	
197	        if (e->tag != TERM_VAR) {
198	            /* Reached an atom binding or an unbound variable — done. */
199	            result.tag      = e->tag;
200	            result.value    = e->value;
201	            result.root_id  = current;
202	            return result;
203	        }
204	
205	        current = e->value.var_id;
206	    }
207	
208	    /* Should be unreachable: cycle would require more entries than capacity. */
209	    assert(0);
210	    return result;
211	}
212	
213	int var_reset(VarRegistry *r)
214	{
215	    if (r == NULL) {
216	        return -1;
217	    }
218	    memset(r, 0, sizeof(VarRegistry));
219	    return 0;
220	}
221	
222	uint32_t var_count(const VarRegistry *r)
223	{
224	    if (r == NULL) {
225	        return 0U;
226	    }
227	    return r->count;
228	}

[300] [assistant_text] The REPL is running but returning false on the grandparent query. The issue is that after loading clauses via the REPL loop, the `TermArena` and `VarRegistry` get reset for each new clause (they're shared state). The clause terms' `TermRef`s point into the arena — but when `run_query` calls `term_arena_init` it wipes the arena, invalidating the stored refs in the database. The database stores `TermRef`s (indices) that are now invalid.

The fix: use a **persistent arena** for asserted clauses and a separate **query-scoped arena** for each query. Let me restructure `ReplState` to separate them:

2	 * repl.h — Read-Eval-Print Loop for Epilog.

102	static void run_query(ReplState *s, const char *src)
103	{
104	    static TokenList      tokens; /* 128 KB — too large for stack */
105	    static SolutionContext ctx;   /* var name table — too large for stack */
106	    Parser    parser;
107	    ParsedQuery pq;
108	    SolverEnv   env;
109	    uint32_t    solutions;
110	    uint32_t    i;
111	
112	    memset(&tokens, 0, sizeof(tokens));
113	    memset(&ctx,    0, sizeof(ctx));
114	
115	    if (lex(src, &tokens) < 0) {
116	        fprintf(stderr, "lex error\n");
117	        return;
118	    }
119	
120	    /* reset per-query arena and vars */
121	    term_arena_init(&s->arena);
122	    var_registry_init(&s->vars);
123	
124	    if (parser_init(&parser, &tokens, &s->arena, &s->atoms, &s->vars) < 0) {
125	        return;
126	    }
127	
128	    if (parser_query(&parser, &pq) != PARSE_OK) {
129	        fprintf(stderr, "parse error: %s\n", parser.error_msg);
130	        return;
131	    }
132	
133	    /* record query variable names for printing */
134	    ctx.arena           = &s->arena;
135	    ctx.vars            = &s->vars;
136	    ctx.atoms           = &s->atoms;
137	    ctx.query_var_count = s->vars.count;
138	    for (i = 0U; i < s->vars.count && i < VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY; ++i) {
139	        ctx.query_vars[i] = s->vars.entries[i].id;
140	        memcpy(ctx.query_var_names[i], s->vars.entries[i].name,
141	               VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN + 1U);
142	    }
143	
144	    if (solver_env_init(&env, &s->arena, &s->vars, &s->atoms, &s->db) < 0) {
145	        return;
146	    }
147	
148	    solutions = solve(&env, pq.goals, pq.goal_count, print_solution, &ctx);
149	
150	    if (solutions == 0U) {
151	        printf("false.\n");
152	    } else {
153	        printf("\n");
154	    }
155	}

158	 * load_clauses — parse a source buffer and assert all clauses.

161	static int load_clauses(ReplState *s, const char *src, const char *label)
162	{
[313] [tool_result]  RESULT: The file d:\Epilog\repl.c has been updated successfully. (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)
[316] [tool_result]  RESULT: 162	
163	static int load_clauses(ReplState *s, const char *src, const char *label)
164	{
165	    static TokenList tokens; /* 128 KB — too large for stack */
166	    Parser       parser;
167	    ParsedClause clause;
168	    ParseResult  pr;
169	    int          loaded = 0;
170	
171	    memset(&tokens, 0, sizeof(tokens));
172	
173	    if (lex(src, &tokens) < 0) {
174	        fprintf(stderr, "%s: lex error\n", label);
175	        return -1;
176	    }
177	
178	    term_arena_init(&s->arena);
179	    var_registry_init(&s->vars);
180	
181	    if (parser_init(&parser, &tokens, &s->arena, &s->atoms, &s->vars) < 0) {
182	        return -1;
183	    }
184	
185	    for (;;) {
186	        memset(&clause, 0, sizeof(ParsedClause));
187	        pr = parser_next_clause(&parser, &clause);
188	        if (pr == PARSE_EOF) { break; }
189	        if (pr != PARSE_OK) {
190	            fprintf(stderr, "%s: parse error: %s\n", label, parser.error_msg);
191	            return -1;
192	        }
193	        if (db_assert_last(&s->db, &clause, &s->arena) < 0) {
194	            fprintf(stderr, "%s: database full\n", label);
195	            return -1;
196	        }
197	        loaded++;
198	    }
199	    return loaded;
200	}

206	int repl_init(ReplState *s)
207	{
208	    if (s == NULL) { return -1; }
209	    if (atom_table_init(&s->atoms) < 0) { return -1; }
210	    if (db_init(&s->db)            < 0) { return -1; }
211	    if (term_arena_init(&s->arena) < 0) { return -1; }
212	    if (var_registry_init(&s->vars)< 0) { return -1; }
213	    return 0;
214	}

[476] [assistant_text] The issue is the REPL loops clause-by-clause in `load_clauses`, and after loading each clause it calls `var_registry_init(&s->clause_vars)` to reset. But the VarIds in the stored `TermRef`s inside `clause_arena` still refer to those old (now zeroed-out) VarIds. The root architectural problem: storing `VarId` in the Term and then trying to bind those VarIds via a query-scoped registry. The clause-time VarIds are just local to the parse — after `rename_term` they become fresh query-time VarIds allocated in `env->vars`. The clause_arena term nodes themselves are just blueprints, never bound directly. So as long as `rename_term` correctly reads the clause var name from `t->data.var.name` and allocates a fresh VarId in `env->vars`, the clause VarIds are never used for binding.

But `parser_next_clause` internally calls `var_reset(p->vars)` for each clause. So between clauses, clause_vars gets reset inside the parser loop. That's fine for isolation. The TermRefs from the arena are stable.

[482] [tool_result]  RESULT: 49	    }

52	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
53	 * deref — follow var→var chains to the root term.
54	 * Bounded by VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY (JPL Rule 2).
55	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
56	
57	TermRef deref(TermRef ref, const TermArena *arena, const VarRegistry *vars)
58	{
59	    uint32_t steps;
60	
61	    if (ref == TERM_REF_INVALID) { return TERM_REF_INVALID; }
62	
63	    for (steps = 0U; steps < VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY; ++steps) {
64	        const Term *t = term_get(arena, ref);
65	        if (t == NULL) { return TERM_REF_INVALID; }
66	
67	        if (t->kind != TERM_KIND_VAR) { return ref; }
68	
69	        /* variable: check if bound */
70	        {
71	            WalkResult wr = var_walk(vars, t->data.var.var_id);
72	            if (wr.tag == TERM_UNBOUND) { return ref; }
73	            if (wr.tag == TERM_ATOM) {
74	                /* bound to an atom — need its TermRef; return self */
75	                return ref;
76	            }
77	            if (wr.tag == TERM_VAR) {
78	                /* bound to another variable — follow */
79	                /* find TermRef for wr.root_id */
80	                uint32_t ni;
81	                for (ni = 0U; ni < arena->count; ++ni) {
82	                    const Term *candidate = &arena->nodes[ni];
83	                    if (candidate->kind == TERM_KIND_VAR &&
84	                        candidate->data.var.var_id == wr.root_id) {
85	                        ref = candidate->ref;
86	                        break;
87	                    }
88	                }
89	            }
90	        }
91	    }
92	    return ref;
93	}
[483] [assistant_text] Found it. `deref` is called from `unify` with just `env->arena` (query arena), but when unifying the query goal against the freshly renamed head, the query goal's vars are in query arena and the renamed head's vars are also in query arena (since `rename_term` writes to `env->arena`). That's consistent.

But in `unify` (solver.c) there's `bind_var` for compound/int targets that does a self-bind `var_bind_var(vars, vid, vid)` — that makes the var "bound to itself" which `var_walk` will return `TERM_UNBOUND` for (since `id == other_id` is a no-op). That means compound terms never get their variable bindings properly stored. This is the bug.

The unifier can only store `TERM_ATOM` or `TERM_VAR` bindings in `VarRegistry`. For `TERM_COMPOUND` and `TERM_INT` targets, binding needs a different approach. The cleanest fix for 0.1: bind a var to another var that points to the compound term — but we need to store the TermRef somewhere. The standard Prolog solution is to store TermRefs directly in the binding, not atoms/vars. Let me refactor `VarRegistry` binding to store a `TermRef` directly:

This is a deep architectural change. The faster fix for 0.1: replace `VarRegistry` bindings with a flat `TermRef` array — each `VarId` maps to the `TermRef` it's bound to (or `TERM_REF_INVALID` if unbound). This is simpler and correct:
[485] [tool_result]  RESULT: 38	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
39	
40	/*
41	 * VarId — opaque handle. VAR_ID_INVALID signals absence / error.
42	 */
43	typedef uint32_t VarId;
44	#define VAR_ID_INVALID  0U
45	#define VAR_ID_MIN      1U
46	
47	/*
48	 * TermTag — discriminates what a bound variable holds.
49	 */
50	typedef enum {
51	    TERM_UNBOUND = 0, /* variable is free          */
52	    TERM_ATOM,        /* bound to an interned atom  */
53	    TERM_VAR          /* bound to another variable  */
54	} TermTag;
55	
56	/*
57	 * Binding — the value slot inside a VarEntry.
58	 * Union kept flat so pointer depth never exceeds 1.
59	 */
60	typedef union {
61	    AtomId atom_id; /* valid when tag == TERM_ATOM */
62	    VarId  var_id;  /* valid when tag == TERM_VAR  */
63	} BindingValue;
64	
65	/*
66	 * VarEntry — one slot in the registry.
67	 */
68	typedef struct {
69	    char         name[VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN + 1U]; /* source-level name, may be "_" */
70	    TermTag      tag;
71	    BindingValue value;
72	    VarId        id; /* == slot index + VAR_ID_MIN */
73	} VarEntry;
74	
75	/*
76	 * VarRegistry — fixed-size store for one query's variables.
77	 * Allocate as a local or static; do NOT heap-alloc.
78	 */
79	typedef struct {
80	    VarEntry entries[VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY];
81	    uint32_t count;
82	} VarRegistry;
83	
84	/*
85	 * WalkResult — return type for var_walk.
86	 */
87	typedef struct {
88	    TermTag      tag;
89	    BindingValue value;    /* meaningful when tag != TERM_UNBOUND */
90	    VarId        root_id;  /* the canonical (deepest bound) VarId */
91	} WalkResult;
92	
[490] [assistant_text] Rather than restructuring `VarRegistry` (which would cascade through all the other files), the simpler fix is to change the binding model: bind variables to a `TermRef` directly. I'll add `TERM_REF` as a binding tag and store a `TermRef` in the union. This requires minimal changes to the public API:

138	int var_bind_var(VarRegistry *r, VarId id, VarId other_id)
139	{
140	    VarEntry *e;
[505] [tool_result]  RESULT: 160	    if (e->tag != TERM_UNBOUND) {
161	        return -1; /* already bound */
162	    }
163	
164	    e->tag          = TERM_VAR;
165	    e->value.var_id = other_id;

168	
169	WalkResult var_walk(const VarRegistry *r, VarId id)
170	{
171	    WalkResult     result;
172	    const VarEntry *e;
173	    VarId           current;
174	    uint32_t        steps;
175	
176	    result.tag            = TERM_UNBOUND;
177	    result.value.var_id   = VAR_ID_INVALID;
178	    result.root_id        = VAR_ID_INVALID;
179	
180	    if (r == NULL || id == VAR_ID_INVALID) {
181	        return result;
182	    }
183	
184	    current = id;
185	
186	    /*
187	     * Follow the binding chain. Bounded by VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY so the
188	     * loop is statically provable to terminate (JPL Rule 2).
189	     * No path compression — keeps pointer depth ≤ 1 (JPL Rule 7).
190	     */
191	    for (steps = 0U; steps < VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY; ++steps) {
192	        e = entry_at_c(r, current);
193	        if (e == NULL) {
194	            return result; /* dangling id — return invalid */
195	        }
196	
197	        if (e->tag != TERM_VAR) {
198	            /* Reached an atom binding or an unbound variable — done. */
199	            result.tag      = e->tag;
200	            result.value    = e->value;
201	            result.root_id  = current;
202	            return result;
203	        }
204	
205	        current = e->value.var_id;
206	    }
207	
208	    /* Should be unreachable: cycle would require more entries than capacity. */
209	    assert(0);
210	    return result;
211	}

100	static int bind_var(VarId vid, TermRef target_ref, const TermArena *arena,
101	                    VarRegistry *vars, Trail *trail)
102	{
103	    const Term *target;
104	
105	    assert(vars != NULL && trail != NULL && arena != NULL);
106	
107	    if (trail->top >= TRAIL_CAPACITY) { return -1; }
108

113	        if (var_bind_atom(vars, vid, target->data.atom_id) < 0) { return -1; }
114	    } else if (target->kind == TERM_KIND_VAR) {
115	        if (var_bind_var(vars, vid, target->data.var.var_id) < 0) { return -1; }

120	        }
121	        /* For compound/int targets we bind the var to itself and let the
122	         * compound's TermRef be used directly by the solver. This is safe
123	         * because TermRefs are stable indices into the arena. */
124	        (void)var_bind_atom; /* suppress unused warning */
125	    }
126	
127	    trail->bound[trail->top] = vid;
128	    trail->top++;
129	    return 0;
130	}

[550] [assistant_text] Now add the global definition and `LOG_INIT` call to main.c, and wire VLOG into the solver's critical path so we can see the trace:
[552] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * main.c — Entry point for the Epilog logic programming runtime.
3	 *
4	 * Usage:
5	 *   epilog                    — start interactive REPL
6	 *   epilog file.pl            — load file then start REPL
7	 *   epilog file.pl -g "goal." — load file, run goal, exit
8	 *
9	 * JPL rules: no recursion, bounded loops, no post-init malloc.
10	 */
11	
12	#include "include/repl.h"
13	#include <stdio.h>
14	#include <string.h>
15	
16	int main(int argc, char *argv[])
17	{
18	    static ReplState s;  /* too large for the stack (~4 MB) */
19	    int              i;
20	
21	    if (repl_init(&s) < 0) {
22	        fprintf(stderr, "epilog: failed to initialise runtime\n");
23	        return 1;
24	    }
25	
26	    /* load any .pl files passed as arguments before '-g' */
27	    for (i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {
28	        if (strncmp(argv[i], "-g", 3) == 0) { break; }
29	        if (repl_load_file(&s, argv[i]) < 0) { return 1; }
30	    }
31	
32	    /* if '-g goal.' was given, run it and exit */
33	    for (i = 1; i < argc - 1; ++i) {
34	        if (strncmp(argv[i], "-g", 3) == 0) {
35	            /* wrap in '?- goal.' if not already a query */
36	            char query[REPL_LINE_MAX];
37	            const char *arg = argv[i + 1];
38	            if (arg[0] == '?' && arg[1] == '-') {
39	                /* already a query string */
40	                repl_run(&s); /* not ideal but safe */
41	            } else {
42	                int n = snprintf(query, sizeof(query), "?- %s", arg);
43	                if (n < 0 || (size_t)n >= sizeof(query)) {
44	                    fprintf(stderr, "epilog: -g argument too long\n");
45	                    return 1;
46	                }
47	                /* ensure trailing dot */
48	                if (query[n - 1] != '.') {
49	                    if ((size_t)n + 2U < sizeof(query)) {
50	                        query[n]     = '.';
51	                        query[n + 1] = '\0';
52	                    }
53	                }
54	                /* parse and run as a file of one query */
55	                /* simplest path: feed through repl_load_file logic */
56	                /* For 0.1 we just start the REPL; -g is future work */
57	                fprintf(stderr,
58	                        "epilog: -g not yet supported; starting REPL\n");
59	                repl_run(&s);
60	            }
61	            return 0;
62	        }
63	    }
64	
65	    repl_run(&s);
66	    return 0;
67	}

39	                /* already a query string */
40	                repl_run(&s); /* not ideal but safe */
41	            } else {
42	                int n = snprintf(query, sizeof(query), "?- %s", arg);
43	                if (n < 0 || (size_t)n >= sizeof(query)) {
44	                    fprintf(stderr, "epilog: -g argument too long\n");
45	                    return 1;
46	                }
47	                /* ensure trailing dot */
48	                if (query[n - 1] != '.') {
49	                    if ((size_t)n + 2U < sizeof(query)) {
50	                        query[n]     = '.';
51	                        query[n + 1] = '\0';
52	                    }
53	                }
54	                /* parse and run as a file of one query */
55	                /* simplest path: feed through repl_load_file logic */
56	                /* For 0.1 we just start the REPL; -g is future work */
57	                fprintf(stderr,
58	                        "epilog: -g not yet supported; starting REPL\n");
59	                repl_run(&s);
60	            }
61	            return 0;
62	        }
63	    }
64	
65	    repl_run(&s);
66	    return 0;
67	}

[557] [assistant_text] Now add `VLOG_SUBSYS` calls to the solver's critical path so we can trace the bug:
[559] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * solver.c — Backtracking SLD resolution engine.
3	 *
4	 * Execution is iterative over an explicit goal stack and choice-point stack.
5	 * No C-stack recursion (JPL Rule 1). All stacks are bounded (JPL Rule 2).
6	 *
7	 * Algorithm per iteration:
8	 *   1. Pop the top goal.
9	 *   2. Deref it to find functor/arity.
10	 *   3. If it is a built-in, dispatch and handle result.
11	 *   4. Otherwise, look up matching clauses in the database.
12	 *   5. If multiple clauses match, push a ChoicePoint for all but the first.
13	 *   6. Unify goal with clause head; push body goals (reversed) onto goal stack.
14	 *   7. On failure, pop the most recent ChoicePoint and restore state.
15	 *
16	 * Variable scoping: each clause application must use fresh variables.
17	 * We achieve this by copying clause terms into a per-application TermArena
18	 * region and renaming variables — implemented as a "variant" step that
19	 * allocates new VarIds for each clause body instantiation.
20	 */
21	
22	#include "../include/solver.h"
23	#include "../include/builtins.h"
24	#include <string.h>
25	#include <assert.h>

The unify pushes the pair `(dgoal, fresh_head)`. Then it deref's both sides: the left is a compound (stays compound), the right is a compound (stays compound). Both are `parent/2`. It then pushes pairs of args: `(arg0_left, arg0_right)` and `(arg1_left, arg1_right)`.

`arg0_left` = TermRef of `fresh_Y` var in env->arena.  
`arg0_right` = TermRef of `fresh_alice` atom in env->arena.

`deref(fresh_Y_ref, env->arena, env->vars)`:
- `t->kind == TERM_KIND_VAR`, `var_id = fresh_Y_vid`
- `var_walk(vars, fresh_Y_vid)` → should return `TERM_TERMREF` since `fresh_Y` was bound to the fresh_alice atom TermRef

Wait — was `fresh_Y` bound to an atom or a TermRef? During head unification: `child(fresh_X', fresh_Y')` unifies with `child(bob_ref, alice_ref)`. The bob_ref and alice_ref in the query goal are atom term nodes. `bind_var(fresh_X'_vid, bob_ref, ...)` → `target->kind == TERM_KIND_ATOM` → `var_bind_atom(vars, fresh_X'_vid, bob_atom_id)`. So `fresh_Y' → var_bind_atom → TERM_ATOM`.

Then in `deref(fresh_Y'_ref)`:
- `var_walk → tag=TERM_ATOM, value.atom_id=alice_id`
- Code says: `if (wr.tag == TERM_ATOM) { return ref; }` — returns the var's own ref!

Then in unify, left side = `fresh_Y'_ref` (var bound to atom), right side = `fresh_alice_ref` (new atom node). After deref, left is still the VAR ref (because deref returns `ref` for TERM_ATOM). Then the unify code:

```c
if (ta->kind == TERM_KIND_VAR) {
    bind_var(ta->data.var.var_id, rb, ...)  // tries to bind already-bound var!
}
```

That fails because `fresh_Y'` is already bound. **This is the bug.** `deref` for TERM_ATOM should **not** return the var ref — it should return a ref to the actual atom term, or the caller must handle atom-bound vars specially.

The fix: when `var_walk` returns `TERM_ATOM`, create an atom term in the arena and return its ref — or better, scan the arena for an existing atom term with that atom_id. Actually the cleanest fix is: `deref` for TERM_ATOM should look for the atom term in the arena, or we should just use `TERM_TERMREF` for atoms too so `deref` can follow it directly.

The simplest fix: change `var_bind_atom` to always use `var_bind_term` with an atom TermRef. But we'd need the TermRef at bind time — which we have (`target_ref`). Let me just remove the TERM_ATOM fast path in `bind_var` and always use `var_bind_term`:

60	{

62	    AtomId       aid;
63	    VarId        vid;

65	
66	    t = peek(p);
67	
68	    /* integer */
69	    if (t->kind == TOK_INT) {
70	        advance(p);
71	        return term_make_int(p->arena, t->int_val);
72	    }
73	
74	    /* variable */
75	    if (t->kind == TOK_VAR) {
76	        advance(p);
77	        /* re-use existing variable with same name in this clause */
78	        vid = VAR_ID_INVALID;
79	        {
80	            uint32_t n;
81	            for (n = 0U; n < var_count(p->vars); ++n) {
82	                VarId probe = (VarId)(n + VAR_ID_MIN);
83	                WalkResult wr = var_walk(p->vars, probe);
84	                /* check name via the registry's entries directly */
85	                (void)wr;
86	            }
87	            /* simpler: scan by position */
88	            {
89	                uint32_t k;
90	                for (k = 0U; k < p->vars->count; ++k) {
91	                    if (strncmp(p->vars->entries[k].name, t->text,

95	                    }
96	                }
97	            }
98	        }
99	        if (vid == VAR_ID_INVALID) {
100	            vid = var_alloc(p->vars, t->text);
101	        }
102	        if (vid == VAR_ID_INVALID) {
103	            snprintf(p->error_msg, sizeof(p->error_msg),
104	                     "line %u: variable registry full", t->line);
105	            return TERM_REF_INVALID;

108	    }
109	
110	    /* cut — treat as atom '!' */

112	        advance(p);

114	        if (aid == ATOM_ID_INVALID) { return TERM_REF_INVALID; }
115	        return term_make_atom(p->arena, aid);
116	    }
117

120	        const char *name = t->text;
121	        advance(p);
122	        aid = atom_intern(p->atoms, name);

126	            return TERM_REF_INVALID;
127	        }

131	            TermRef args[TERM_MAX_ARITY];
132	            uint8_t arity = 0U;
133	
134	            advance(p); /* consume '(' */
135	
136	            if (peek(p)->kind != TOK_RPAREN) {
137	                /* parse comma-separated arg list */
138	                uint32_t a;

[685] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * variables.h — Logic variable registry for the Epilog inference engine.
3	 *
4	 * A logic variable is an unbound or bound slot in a unification frame.
5	 * Variables are identified by a VarId scoped to a VarRegistry that lives
6	 * for the duration of a single query. The registry is reset between queries;
7	 * no heap allocation occurs after init.
8	 *
9	 * Binding is represented as a union-find structure without path compression
10	 * to keep pointer depth ≤ 1 and behaviour fully predictable (JPL rule 7).
11	 * Binding chains are therefore at most VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY long, which is
12	 * statically bounded (JPL rule 2).
13	 *
14	 * JPL rules observed:
15	 *   - No dynamic allocation after var_registry_init().
16	 *   - All loops have a statically provable upper bound.
17	 *   - No recursion — walk() is an explicit bounded loop.
18	 *   - No more than one level of pointer indirection in public API.
19	 *   - Every function checks its parameters; callers must check returns.
20	 */
21	
22	#ifndef EPILOG_VARIABLES_H
23	#define EPILOG_VARIABLES_H
24	
25	#include <stddef.h>    /* size_t   */
26	#include <stdint.h>    /* uint32_t */
27	#include "atoms.h"     /* AtomId   */
28	
29	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
30	 * Compile-time limits
31	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
32	
33	#define VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY  1024U  /* max variables per query frame */
34	#define VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN         63U  /* max chars in a variable name  */
35	
36	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
37	 * Public types
38	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
39	
40	/*
41	 * VarId — opaque handle. VAR_ID_INVALID signals absence / error.
42	 */
43	typedef uint32_t VarId;
44	#define VAR_ID_INVALID  0U
45	#define VAR_ID_MIN      1U
46	
47	/* Forward declaration — TermRef is uint32_t defined in terms.h.
48	 * We avoid including terms.h here to prevent circular dependencies. */
49	typedef uint32_t TermRef;
50	
51	/*
52	 * TermTag — discriminates what a bound variable holds.
53	 */
54	typedef enum {
55	    TERM_UNBOUND = 0, /* variable is free                      */
56	    TERM_ATOM,        /* bound to an interned atom (atom_id)   */
57	    TERM_VAR,         /* bound to another variable (var_id)    */
58	    TERM_TERMREF      /* bound to any term via TermRef         */
59	} TermTag;
60	
61	/*
62	 * Binding — the value slot inside a VarEntry.
63	 * Union kept flat so pointer depth never exceeds 1.
64	 */
65	typedef union {
66	    AtomId  atom_id; /* valid when tag == TERM_ATOM    */
67	    VarId   var_id;  /* valid when tag == TERM_VAR     */
68	    TermRef term_ref;/* valid when tag == TERM_TERMREF */
69	} BindingValue;
70	
71	/*
72	 * VarEntry — one slot in the registry.
73	 */
74	typedef struct {
75	    char         name[VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN + 1U]; /* source-level name, may be "_" */
76	    TermTag      tag;
77	    BindingValue value;
78	    VarId        id; /* == slot index + VAR_ID_MIN */
79	} VarEntry;
80	
81	/*
82	 * VarRegistry — fixed-size store for one query's variables.
83	 * Allocate as a local or static; do NOT heap-alloc.
84	 */
85	typedef struct {
86	    VarEntry entries[VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY];
87	    uint32_t count;
88	} VarRegistry;
89	
90	/*
91	 * WalkResult — return type for var_walk.
92	 */
93	typedef struct {
94	    TermTag      tag;
95	    BindingValue value;    /* meaningful when tag != TERM_UNBOUND */
96	    VarId        root_id;  /* the canonical (deepest bound) VarId */
97	} WalkResult;
98	
99	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
100	 * Public API
101	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
102	
103	/*
104	 * var_registry_init — zero-initialise *r before first use.
105	 * Must be called once per query, before any other var_* function.
106	 * Returns 0 on success, -1 if r is NULL.
107	 */
108	int var_registry_init(VarRegistry *r);
109	
110	/*
111	 * var_alloc — allocate a fresh unbound variable named *name.
112	 * name must be NUL-terminated and ≤ VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN chars.
113	 * Returns VAR_ID_INVALID if the registry is full or name is NULL.
114	 */
115	VarId var_alloc(VarRegistry *r, const char *name);
116	
117	/*
118	 * var_bind_atom — bind variable id to atom atom_id.
119	 * Fails (returns -1) if id is already bound or either argument is invalid.
120	 * Returns 0 on success.
121	 */
122	int var_bind_atom(VarRegistry *r, VarId id, AtomId atom_id);
123	
124	/*
125	 * var_bind_var — bind variable id to another variable other_id.
126	 * Fails (returns -1) if id is already bound or either id is invalid.
127	 * Returns 0 on success.
128	 */
129	int var_bind_var(VarRegistry *r, VarId id, VarId other_id);
130	
131	/*
132	 * var_bind_term — bind variable id to an arbitrary term (TermRef).
133	 * Used when a variable unifies with a compound or integer term.
134	 * Returns 0 on success, -1 if id is already bound or arguments invalid.
135	 */
136	int var_bind_term(VarRegistry *r, VarId id, TermRef ref);
137	
138	/*
139	 * var_walk — follow binding chain from id to its canonical root.
140	 * Terminates in at most VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY steps (bounded loop, no
141	 * recursion). Returns a WalkResult describing the final state.
142	 * On invalid id, result.tag == TERM_UNBOUND and result.root_id == VAR_ID_INVALID.
143	 */
144	WalkResult var_walk(const VarRegistry *r, VarId id);
145	
146	/*
147	 * var_reset — clear all bindings, keeping capacity intact.
148	 * Equivalent to re-running var_registry_init on an existing registry.
149	 * Returns 0 on success, -1 if r is NULL.
150	 */
151	int var_reset(VarRegistry *r);
152	
153	/*
154	 * var_count — number of variables currently allocated in *r.
155	 */
156	uint32_t var_count(const VarRegistry *r);
157	
158	#endif /* EPILOG_VARIABLES_H */
159	
[687] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * terms.h — Term representation for the Epilog inference engine.
3	 *
4	 * A Term is the universal data type of logic programming:
5	 *   - Atom       : an interned string constant  (e.g. alice, foo, [])
6	 *   - Var        : a logic variable             (e.g. X, _G42)
7	 *   - Compound   : a functor + fixed arity args (e.g. parent(alice,bob))
8	 *   - Int        : a machine integer            (e.g. 42)
9	 *
10	 * Storage strategy — no heap after init (JPL Rule 3):
11	 *   All terms live in a TermArena whose backing array is stack- or
12	 *   statically-allocated.  A TermRef is a 32-bit index into that arena.
13	 *   Compound arguments are stored as a contiguous run of TermRefs beginning
14	 *   at arg_start inside a shared ArgPool.
15	 *
16	 * JPL rules observed:
17	 *   Rule 1  — no goto, no recursion.
18	 *   Rule 2  — all loops and capacities statically bounded.
19	 *   Rule 3  — no malloc/free after arena init.
20	 *   Rule 5  — every function validates parameters, returns error codes.
21	 *   Rule 6  — preprocessor limited to includes + constants.
22	 *   Rule 7  — pointer depth ≤ 1 in public API.
23	 */
24	
25	#ifndef EPILOG_TERMS_H
26	#define EPILOG_TERMS_H
27	
28	#include <stdint.h>
29	#include <stddef.h>
30	#include "atoms.h"
31	#include "variables.h"
32	
33	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
34	 * Capacities
35	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
36	
37	#define TERM_ARENA_CAPACITY   8192U   /* max terms per arena          */
38	#define ARG_POOL_CAPACITY    32768U   /* max compound-arg slots total  */
39	#define TERM_MAX_ARITY          255U  /* max arguments for a compound  */
40	#define TERM_REF_INVALID          0U  /* sentinel — no valid term       */
41	
42	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
43	 * TermRef — opaque index into a TermArena.
44	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
45	
46	typedef uint32_t TermRef;
47	
48	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
49	 * TermKind — discriminator tag.
50	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
51	
52	typedef enum {
53	    TERM_KIND_ATOM     = 1,
54	    TERM_KIND_VAR      = 2,
55	    TERM_KIND_COMPOUND = 3,
56	    TERM_KIND_INT      = 4
57	} TermKind;
58	
59	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
60	 * Term — one node stored inside the arena.
61	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
62	
63	typedef struct {
64	    TermKind kind;
65	    union {
66	        AtomId   atom_id;    /* TERM_KIND_ATOM                           */
67	        int32_t  int_val;    /* TERM_KIND_INT                            */
68	        struct {             /* TERM_KIND_VAR                            */
69	            VarId var_id;
70	            char  name[VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN + 1U]; /* stored for renaming   */
71	        } var;
72	        struct {             /* TERM_KIND_COMPOUND                       */
73	            AtomId   functor;    /* functor name                         */
74	            uint8_t  arity;      /* number of arguments                  */
75	            uint32_t arg_start;  /* index into ArgPool                   */
76	        } compound;
77	    } data;
78	    TermRef ref; /* self-index; set on allocation */
79	} Term;
80	
81	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
82	 * ArgPool — flat array of TermRefs used as compound arg storage.
83	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
84	
85	typedef struct {
86	    TermRef  slots[ARG_POOL_CAPACITY];
87	    uint32_t count;
88	} ArgPool;
89	
90	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
91	 * TermArena — owns all Term nodes for one inference session.
92	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
93	
94	typedef struct {
95	    Term     nodes[TERM_ARENA_CAPACITY];
96	    uint32_t count;
97	    ArgPool  args;
98	} TermArena;
99	
100	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
101	 * Public API
102	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
103	
104	/* term_arena_init — zero-initialise before use. Returns 0 or -1. */
105	int term_arena_init(TermArena *a);
106	
107	/* term_make_atom — allocate an atom term. Returns TERM_REF_INVALID on error. */
108	TermRef term_make_atom(TermArena *a, AtomId id);
109	
110	/* term_make_var — allocate a variable term with its source name.
111	 * name may be NULL (stored as "_"). Returns TERM_REF_INVALID on error. */
112	TermRef term_make_var(TermArena *a, VarId id, const char *name);
113	
114	/* term_make_int — allocate an integer term. Returns TERM_REF_INVALID on error. */
115	TermRef term_make_int(TermArena *a, int32_t val);
116	
117	/*
118	 * term_make_compound — allocate a compound term.
119	 * args is an array of arity TermRefs for the arguments.
120	 * Returns TERM_REF_INVALID on error.
121	 */
122	TermRef term_make_compound(TermArena *a, AtomId functor, uint8_t arity,
123	                            const TermRef *args);
124	
125	/*
126	 * term_get — retrieve a pointer to the Term for ref.
127	 * Returns NULL for TERM_REF_INVALID or out-of-range ref.
128	 * Pointer is valid only while the arena is not modified.
129	 */
130	const Term *term_get(const TermArena *a, TermRef ref);
131	
132	/*
133	 * term_arg — get the n-th argument (0-based) of a compound term.
134	 * Returns TERM_REF_INVALID if ref is not a compound or n >= arity.
135	 */
136	TermRef term_arg(const TermArena *a, TermRef ref, uint8_t n);
137	
138	/* term_kind — return the kind of a term; 0 if ref is invalid. */
139	TermKind term_kind(const TermArena *a, TermRef ref);
140	
141	#endif /* EPILOG_TERMS_H */

[689] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * atoms.h — Atom table for the Epilog inference engine.
3	 *
4	 * An atom is an immutable, interned string. Every unique spelling maps to
5	 * exactly one AtomId. Comparisons are therefore O(1) integer comparisons.
6	 *
7	 * JPL rules observed:
8	 *   - No dynamic allocation after atom_table_init().
9	 *   - All loops have a statically provable upper bound (ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY).
10	 *   - No recursion.
11	 *   - No more than one level of pointer indirection in public API.
12	 *   - Every function documents its preconditions; callers must check returns.
13	 */
14	
15	#ifndef EPILOG_ATOMS_H
16	#define EPILOG_ATOMS_H
17	
18	#include <stddef.h>   /* size_t  */
19	#include <stdint.h>   /* uint32_t */
20	
21	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
22	 * Compile-time limits (Rule 2: fixed upper bounds for all loops/tables).
23	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
24	
25	#define ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY  4096U   /* max distinct atoms     */
26	#define ATOM_MAX_LEN          255U   /* max chars, excl. NUL   */
27	
28	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
29	 * Public types
30	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
31	
32	/*
33	 * AtomId — opaque handle; ATOM_ID_INVALID signals absence / error.
34	 * Zero is intentionally reserved so zero-initialised structs are safe.
35	 */
36	typedef uint32_t AtomId;
37	#define ATOM_ID_INVALID  0U
38	#define ATOM_ID_MIN      1U
39	
40	/* Internal storage for one interned atom. */
41	typedef struct {
42	    char     spelling[ATOM_MAX_LEN + 1U]; /* NUL-terminated              */
43	    uint32_t hash;                         /* pre-computed FNV-1a hash    */
44	    AtomId   id;                           /* == slot index + ATOM_ID_MIN */
45	} Atom;
46	
47	/*
48	 * AtomTable — fixed-size, open-addressed hash table.
49	 * Allocate on the stack or as a module-level static; do NOT heap-alloc.
50	 */
51	typedef struct {
52	    Atom     slots[ATOM_TABLE_CAPACITY];
53	    uint32_t count;   /* number of interned atoms */
54	} AtomTable;
55	
56	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
57	 * Public API
58	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
59	
60	/*
61	 * atom_table_init — zero-initialise *t before first use.
62	 * Must be called exactly once before any other atom_* function.
63	 * Returns 0 on success, -1 if t is NULL.
64	 */
65	int atom_table_init(AtomTable *t);
66	
67	/*
68	 * atom_intern — intern the NUL-terminated string *spelling.
69	 * If an atom with this spelling already exists, returns its id.
70	 * If the table is full or the spelling is too long, returns ATOM_ID_INVALID.
71	 * spelling must not be NULL.
72	 */
73	AtomId atom_intern(AtomTable *t, const char *spelling);
74	
75	/*
76	 * atom_lookup — find an already-interned atom by spelling.
77	 * Returns ATOM_ID_INVALID if not found. Does not insert.
78	 */
79	AtomId atom_lookup(const AtomTable *t, const char *spelling);
80	
81	/*
82	 * atom_spelling — return a pointer to the NUL-terminated spelling of id.
83	 * Returns NULL if id is out of range.
84	 * The pointer is valid for the lifetime of *t; do NOT free it.
85	 */
86	const char *atom_spelling(const AtomTable *t, AtomId id);
87	
88	/*
89	 * atom_count — number of distinct atoms currently interned.
90	 */
91	uint32_t atom_count(const AtomTable *t);
92	
93	#endif /* EPILOG_ATOMS_H */
94	
[691] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * log.h — Verbose logging for the Epilog runtime.
3	 *
4	 * When --verbose or -v is passed, every major step is logged to stderr.
5	 *
6	 * JPL rules: no heap, no recursion, bounded format strings.
7	 */
8	
9	#ifndef EPILOG_LOG_H
10	#define EPILOG_LOG_H
11	
12	#include <stdio.h>
13	#include <string.h>
14	#include <stdarg.h>
15	
16	extern int g_verbose;
17	
18	/* log_init — scan argv for --verbose / -v and set g_verbose. */
19	static inline void log_init(int argc, char *argv[])
20	{
21	    int i;
22	    g_verbose = 0;
23	    for (i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {
24	        if (strncmp(argv[i], "--verbose", 10) == 0 ||
25	            strncmp(argv[i], "-v",         3) == 0) {
26	            g_verbose = 1;
27	        }
28	    }
29	}
30	
31	/* vlog — emit one log line if verbose is on. */
32	static inline void vlog(const char *subsys, const char *fmt, ...)
33	{
34	    va_list ap;
35	    if (!g_verbose) { return; }
36	    fprintf(stderr, "[%s] ", subsys);
37	    va_start(ap, fmt);
38	    vfprintf(stderr, fmt, ap);
39	    va_end(ap);
40	    fputc('\n', stderr);
41	}
42	
43	#define LOG_INIT(argc, argv)          log_init((argc), (argv))
44	#define VLOG(fmt, ...)                vlog("epilog", (fmt), __VA_ARGS__)
45	#define VLOG_SUBSYS(s, fmt, ...)      vlog((s),      (fmt), __VA_ARGS__)
46	
47	#endif /* EPILOG_LOG_H */

[693] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * builtins.h — Built-in predicate dispatch for Epilog 0.1.
3	 *
4	 * Built-ins handled:
5	 *   true/0      — always succeeds
6	 *   fail/0      — always fails
7	 *   !/0         — cut (signals parent choice-point removal)
8	 *   =/2         — unification
9	 *   \=/2        — non-unification
10	 *   write/1     — print a term to stdout
11	 *   nl/0        — print newline
12	 *   assert/1    — add clause to database (assertz semantics)
13	 *   retract/1   — remove first matching clause
14	 *
15	 * Each built-in follows the BuiltinFn signature.
16	 * builtin_lookup returns NULL for unknown functors so the solver falls
17	 * through to user-defined clause resolution.
18	 */

21	#define EPILOG_BUILTINS_H

26	#include "variables.h"
27	#include "unifier.h"
28	#include "database.h"

30	
31	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
32	 * BuiltinResult — what a built-in tells the solver.
33	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
34	
35	typedef enum {
36	    BUILTIN_SUCCEED = 0,   /* goal succeeded; continue            */
37	    BUILTIN_FAIL    = 1,   /* goal failed; backtrack              */
38	    BUILTIN_CUT     = 2,   /* cut; discard parent choice points   */
39	    BUILTIN_ERROR   = 3    /* runtime error; halt with message    */
40	} BuiltinResult;
41	
42	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
43	 * BuiltinFn — function pointer type for all built-in implementations.
44	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */

55	 */
56	BuiltinFn builtin_lookup(AtomId functor, uint8_t arity, const AtomTable *atoms);
57	
58	/*
59	 * term_write — print a term to stdout following standard Prolog notation.
60	 * Used by write/1 and also by the REPL to display solutions.
61	 * Returns 0 on success, -1 on bad args.
62	 */
63	int term_write(TermRef ref, const TermArena *arena,
64	               const VarRegistry *vars, const AtomTable *atoms);

[695] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * lexer.h — Tokenizer for the Epilog source language.
3	 *
4	 * Converts a NUL-terminated source string into a flat array of Token structs.
5	 * No heap allocation: the caller supplies a fixed-size TokenList buffer.
6	 *
7	 * Token classes:
8	 *   TOK_ATOM      lowercase-initial identifier or single-quoted string
9	 *   TOK_VAR       uppercase-initial or underscore-initial identifier
10	 *   TOK_INT       decimal integer
11	 *   TOK_LPAREN    (
12	 *   TOK_RPAREN    )
13	 *   TOK_COMMA     ,
14	 *   TOK_DOT       .  (clause/query terminator)
15	 *   TOK_NECK      :-
16	 *   TOK_QUERY     ?-
17	 *   TOK_PIPE      |
18	 *   TOK_SEMI      ;  (disjunction / "more solutions" prompt)
19	 *   TOK_CUT       !
20	 *   TOK_OP        any other operator token (=, \=, etc.)
21	 *   TOK_EOF       end of input
22	 *   TOK_ERROR     unrecognised character
23	 *
24	 * JPL rules: no recursion, bounded array, no post-init malloc.
25	 */
26	
27	#ifndef EPILOG_LEXER_H
28	#define EPILOG_LEXER_H
29	
30	#include <stdint.h>
31	#include <stddef.h>
32	
33	#define TOKEN_LIST_CAPACITY  2048U
34	#define TOKEN_TEXT_MAX          63U   /* max chars copied into tok.text */
35	
36	typedef enum {
37	    TOK_ATOM = 0,
38	    TOK_VAR,
39	    TOK_INT,
40	    TOK_LPAREN,
41	    TOK_RPAREN,
42	    TOK_COMMA,
43	    TOK_DOT,
44	    TOK_NECK,
45	    TOK_QUERY,
46	    TOK_PIPE,
47	    TOK_SEMI,
48	    TOK_CUT,
49	    TOK_OP,
50	    TOK_EOF,
51	    TOK_ERROR
52	} TokenKind;
53	
54	typedef struct {
55	    TokenKind kind;
56	    char      text[TOKEN_TEXT_MAX + 1U]; /* NUL-terminated lexeme */
57	    int32_t   int_val;                   /* set when kind == TOK_INT */
58	    uint32_t  line;                      /* 1-based source line      */
59	} Token;
60	
61	typedef struct {
62	    Token    items[TOKEN_LIST_CAPACITY];
63	    uint32_t count;
64	} TokenList;
65	
66	/*
67	 * lex — tokenise *src into *out.
68	 * Returns 0 on success, -1 if src or out is NULL.
69	 * On TOK_ERROR the offending character is stored in text[0]; lexing stops.
70	 * TOK_EOF is always the last token appended.
71	 */
72	int lex(const char *src, TokenList *out);
73	
74	/* tok_kind_name — human-readable name for a TokenKind (for error messages). */
75	const char *tok_kind_name(TokenKind k);
76	
77	#endif /* EPILOG_LEXER_H */
78	
[696] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * parser.h — Clause and query parser for Epilog.
3	 *
4	 * Consumes a TokenList (produced by lexer.h) and populates a TermArena with
5	 * the parsed terms.  Results are expressed as ParsedClause / ParsedQuery
6	 * structs that hold TermRefs into the arena.
7	 *
8	 * Grammar (simplified):
9	 *   program   ::= clause* EOF
10	 *   clause    ::= term '.'                    -- fact
11	 *               | term ':-' term_list '.'     -- rule
12	 *   query     ::= '?-' term_list '.'
13	 *   term_list ::= term (',' term)*
14	 *   term      ::= atom
15	 *               | variable
16	 *               | integer
17	 *               | atom '(' term_list ')'      -- compound
18	 *               | term op term                -- operator (= \= only for 0.1)
19	 *               | '!' | 'true' | 'fail'
20	 *
21	 * No heap allocation; caller owns arena and TokenList.
22	 *
23	 * JPL rules: no recursion (iterative descent with explicit stack),
24	 *            bounded loops, no post-init malloc.
25	 */
26	
27	#ifndef EPILOG_PARSER_H
28	#define EPILOG_PARSER_H
29	
30	#include <stdint.h>
31	#include "lexer.h"
32	#include "terms.h"
33	#include "atoms.h"
34	#include "variables.h"
35	
36	#define PARSER_MAX_BODY_GOALS   256U  /* max goals in a clause body    */
37	#define PARSER_MAX_CLAUSES      512U  /* max clauses in one parse pass */
38	
39	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
40	 * ParsedClause
41	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
42	
43	typedef struct {
44	    TermRef head;                              /* always set           */
45	    TermRef body[PARSER_MAX_BODY_GOALS];       /* goals; 0 == fact     */
46	    uint32_t body_len;
47	} ParsedClause;
48	
49	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
50	 * ParsedQuery
51	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
52	
53	typedef struct {
54	    TermRef goals[PARSER_MAX_BODY_GOALS];
55	    uint32_t goal_count;
56	} ParsedQuery;
57	
58	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
59	 * ParseResult — returned by every parse function.
60	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
61	
62	typedef enum {
63	    PARSE_OK = 0,
64	    PARSE_EOF,           /* clean end of token stream              */
65	    PARSE_ERR_SYNTAX,    /* unexpected token                       */
66	    PARSE_ERR_OVERFLOW,  /* clause/body too large, arena full, etc */
67	    PARSE_ERR_ARGS       /* NULL pointer passed                    */
68	} ParseResult;
69	
70	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
71	 * Parser state — initialise with parser_init, then call parse_* functions.
72	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
73	
74	typedef struct {
75	    const TokenList *tokens;
76	    uint32_t         pos;       /* current read position */
77	    TermArena       *arena;
78	    AtomTable       *atoms;
79	    VarRegistry     *vars;      /* reset per clause */
80	    char             error_msg[128];
81	} Parser;
82	
83	/* parser_init — bind a parser to its inputs. Returns 0 or -1. */
84	int parser_init(Parser *p, const TokenList *tokens, TermArena *arena,
85	                AtomTable *atoms, VarRegistry *vars);
86	
87	/*
88	 * parser_next_clause — parse one clause from the token stream.
89	 * Advances p->pos past the terminating '.'.
90	 * Returns PARSE_OK, PARSE_EOF, or an error code.
91	 */
92	ParseResult parser_next_clause(Parser *p, ParsedClause *out);
93	
94	/*
95	 * parser_query — parse a '?- goals .' query.
96	 * Expects the leading TOK_QUERY to be the current token.
97	 */
98	ParseResult parser_query(Parser *p, ParsedQuery *out);
99	
100	#endif /* EPILOG_PARSER_H */
101	
[698] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * database.h — Clause database for the Epilog inference engine.
3	 *
4	 * Stores asserted clauses indexed by functor/arity for fast lookup.
5	 * All storage is pre-allocated in a Database struct (no post-init malloc).
6	 *
7	 * Each predicate (functor + arity pair) owns a contiguous run of ClauseIds
8	 * inside a shared ClausePool.  A PredIndex maps functor/arity → run.
9	 *
10	 * JPL rules: no recursion, bounded capacities, no post-init malloc.
11	 */
12	
13	#ifndef EPILOG_DATABASE_H
14	#define EPILOG_DATABASE_H
15	
16	#include <stdint.h>
17	#include "atoms.h"
18	#include "terms.h"
19	#include "parser.h"
20	
21	#define DB_MAX_PREDICATES   512U
22	#define DB_MAX_CLAUSES     4096U
23	#define DB_MAX_BODY_GOALS   PARSER_MAX_BODY_GOALS
24	
25	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
26	 * Stored clause
27	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
28	
29	typedef uint32_t ClauseId;
30	#define CLAUSE_ID_INVALID  0U
31	
32	typedef struct {
33	    TermRef  head;
34	    TermRef  body[DB_MAX_BODY_GOALS];
35	    uint32_t body_len;
36	    ClauseId id;
37	} StoredClause;
38	
39	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
40	 * Predicate index entry
41	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
42	
43	typedef struct {
44	    AtomId   functor;
45	    uint8_t  arity;
46	    uint32_t first;   /* index into Database.clauses[]  */
47	    uint32_t count;   /* number of clauses for this pred */
48	} PredEntry;
49	
50	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
51	 * Database
52	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
53	
54	typedef struct {
55	    StoredClause clauses[DB_MAX_CLAUSES];
56	    uint32_t     clause_count;
57	
58	    PredEntry    preds[DB_MAX_PREDICATES];
59	    uint32_t     pred_count;
60	} Database;
61	
62	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
63	 * Public API
64	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
65	
66	/* db_init — zero-initialise before use. Returns 0 or -1. */
67	int db_init(Database *db);
68	
69	/*
70	 * db_assert_last — add a clause at the end of its predicate's clause list.
71	 * Returns 0 on success, -1 on capacity overflow or bad args.
72	 */
73	int db_assert_last(Database *db, const ParsedClause *clause, TermArena *arena);
74	
75	/*
76	 * db_retract_first — remove the first matching clause for head.
77	 * Matching is by functor and arity only (caller handles unification).
78	 * Returns 0 on success, -1 if not found.
79	 */
80	int db_retract_first(Database *db, AtomId functor, uint8_t arity);
81	
82	/*
83	 * db_get_pred — find the PredEntry for functor/arity.
84	 * Returns NULL if no clauses are stored for that predicate.
85	 */
86	const PredEntry *db_get_pred(const Database *db, AtomId functor, uint8_t arity);
87	
88	/*
89	 * db_get_clause — retrieve a clause by its index in the clause pool.
90	 * Returns NULL for out-of-range index.
91	 */
92	const StoredClause *db_get_clause(const Database *db, uint32_t idx);
93	
94	#endif /* EPILOG_DATABASE_H */

[700] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * unifier.h — Robinson unification for the Epilog inference engine.
3	 *
4	 * Unification operates on a Trail + VarRegistry pair.
5	 * The Trail records every variable bound during one resolution step so that
6	 * backtracking can undo all bindings in O(trail_length) time.
7	 *
8	 * No occurs check by default (standard Prolog semantics).
9	 *
10	 * JPL rules: no recursion (iterative with explicit work stack),
11	 *            bounded loops, no post-init malloc.
12	 */
13	
14	#ifndef EPILOG_UNIFIER_H
15	#define EPILOG_UNIFIER_H
16	
17	#include <stdint.h>
18	#include "terms.h"
19	#include "variables.h"
20	
21	#define TRAIL_CAPACITY      4096U   /* max bindings recorded per query  */
22	#define UNIFY_STACK_CAPACITY 512U   /* work stack depth for unification  */
23	
24	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
25	 * Trail — records variable bindings for backtrack undo.
26	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
27	
28	typedef struct {
29	    VarId    bound[TRAIL_CAPACITY];
30	    uint32_t top;
31	} Trail;
32	
33	/* trail_init — zero-initialise. Returns 0 or -1. */
34	int trail_init(Trail *t);
35	
36	/* trail_mark — return current trail top (used as a backtrack checkpoint). */
37	uint32_t trail_mark(const Trail *t);
38	
39	/*
40	 * trail_undo — unbind all variables pushed since mark.
41	 * Resets each VarEntry to TERM_UNBOUND.
42	 */
43	void trail_undo(Trail *t, VarRegistry *vars, uint32_t mark);
44	
45	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
46	 * Unification
47	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
48	
49	/*
50	 * unify — attempt to unify terms a and b under the current bindings in vars.
51	 * New bindings are recorded on trail.
52	 * Returns 1 on success (terms unified), 0 on failure (terms clash).
53	 * On failure, bindings made during this call are NOT automatically undone;
54	 * call trail_undo(trail, vars, mark) where mark was taken before the call.
55	 */
56	int unify(TermRef a, TermRef b,
57	          const TermArena *arena, VarRegistry *vars, Trail *trail);
58	
59	/*
60	 * deref — follow variable bindings to the canonical term for ref.
61	 * Returns the TermRef of the root (atom, int, compound, or unbound var).
62	 * Pure read — does not modify vars.
63	 */
64	TermRef deref(TermRef ref, const TermArena *arena, const VarRegistry *vars);
65	
66	#endif /* EPILOG_UNIFIER_H */
