[730] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	/*
2	 * repl.c — Read-Eval-Print Loop.
3	 *
4	 * Reads lines from stdin (or a file), lexes + parses them, and either:
5	 *   - Asserts clauses (when input is a clause ending in '.')
6	 *   - Executes a query (when input starts with '?-')
7	 *   - Loads a file   (when input is 'consult(path).' or ':- consult(path).')
8	 *   - Exits          (when input is 'halt.')
9	 *
10	 * Solution printing: after each solution the REPL prints variable bindings
11	 * and waits for ';' (more) or Enter (stop).
12	 *
13	 * JPL rules: no recursion, bounded buffers, no post-init malloc.
14	 */
15	
16	#include "include/repl.h"
17	#include "include/lexer.h"
18	#include "include/parser.h"
19	#include "include/solver.h"
20	#include "include/builtins.h"
21	
22	#include <stdio.h>
23	#include <string.h>
24	#include <assert.h>
25	
26	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
27	 * SolutionContext — passed through the solver callback.
28	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
29	
30	typedef struct {
31	    const TermArena   *arena;
32	    const VarRegistry *vars;
33	    const AtomTable   *atoms;
34	    /* variable names from the query (for printing) */
35	    VarId   query_vars[VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY];
36	    char    query_var_names[VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY][VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN + 1U];
37	    uint32_t query_var_count;
38	    /* user chose to stop? */
39	    int stop;
40	} SolutionContext;
41	
42	/* print_solution — display bindings and prompt for more. */
43	static SolveSignal print_solution(SolverEnv *env,
44	                                  const TermRef *goals, uint32_t goal_count,
45	                                  void *userdata)
46	{
47	    SolutionContext *ctx = (SolutionContext *)userdata;
48	    uint32_t i;
49	    int      any_binding = 0;
50	
51	    (void)goals; (void)goal_count;
52	
53	    for (i = 0U; i < ctx->query_var_count; ++i) {
54	        VarId      vid  = ctx->query_vars[i];
55	        const char *name = ctx->query_var_names[i];
56	
57	        /* skip anonymous variables */
58	        if (name[0] == '_' && name[1] == '\0') { continue; }
59	
60	        /* find the TermRef for this var in the current arena */
61	        {
62	            uint32_t ni;
63	            for (ni = 0U; ni < env->arena->count; ++ni) {
64	                const Term *t = &env->arena->nodes[ni];
65	                if (t->kind == TERM_KIND_VAR && t->data.var.var_id == vid) {
66	                    printf("%s = ", name);
67	                    term_write(t->ref, env->arena, env->vars, env->atoms);
68	                    printf("\n");
69	                    any_binding = 1;
70	                    break;
71	                }
72	            }
73	        }
74	    }
75	
76	    if (!any_binding) {
77	        printf("true");
78	    }
79	
80	    /* prompt for more */
81	    printf(" ; ");
82	    fflush(stdout);
83	    {
84	        char ch = (char)getchar();
85	        if (ch == ';') {
86	            /* consume rest of line */
87	            while (ch != '\n' && ch != (char)EOF) { ch = (char)getchar(); }
88	            return SOLVE_NEXT;
89	        }
90	        /* any other key — stop */
91	        if (ch != '\n') {
92	            while (ch != '\n' && ch != (char)EOF) { ch = (char)getchar(); }
93	        }
94	    }
95	    return SOLVE_STOP;
96	}
97	
98	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
99	 * run_query — lex, parse, and execute one query string.
100	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
101	
102	static void run_query(ReplState *s, const char *src)
103	{
104	    static TokenList      tokens; /* 128 KB — too large for stack */
105	    static SolutionContext ctx;   /* var name table — too large for stack */
106	    Parser    parser;
107	    ParsedQuery pq;
108	    SolverEnv   env;
109	    uint32_t    solutions;
110	    uint32_t    i;
111	
112	    memset(&tokens, 0, sizeof(tokens));
113	    memset(&ctx,    0, sizeof(ctx));
114	
115	    if (lex(src, &tokens) < 0) {
116	        fprintf(stderr, "lex error\n");
117	        return;
118	    }
119	
120	    /* reset per-query arena and vars; clause_arena is untouched */
121	    term_arena_init(&s->query_arena);
122	    var_registry_init(&s->query_vars);
123	
124	    if (parser_init(&parser, &tokens, &s->query_arena,
125	                    &s->atoms, &s->query_vars) < 0) {
126	        return;
127	    }
128	
129	    if (parser_query(&parser, &pq) != PARSE_OK) {
130	        fprintf(stderr, "parse error: %s\n", parser.error_msg);
131	        return;
132	    }
133	
134	    /* record query variable names for printing */
135	    ctx.arena           = &s->query_arena;
136	    ctx.vars            = &s->query_vars;
137	    ctx.atoms           = &s->atoms;
138	    ctx.query_var_count = s->query_vars.count;
139	    for (i = 0U; i < s->query_vars.count && i < VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY; ++i) {
140	        ctx.query_vars[i] = s->query_vars.entries[i].id;
141	        memcpy(ctx.query_var_names[i], s->query_vars.entries[i].name,
142	               VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN + 1U);
143	    }
144	
145	    if (solver_env_init(&env, &s->query_arena, &s->clause_arena,
146	                        &s->query_vars, &s->clause_vars,
147	                        &s->atoms, &s->db) < 0) {
148	        return;
149	    }
150	
151	    solutions = solve(&env, pq.goals, pq.goal_count, print_solution, &ctx);
152	
153	    if (solutions == 0U) {
154	        printf("false.\n");
155	    } else {
156	        printf("\n");
157	    }
158	}
159	
160	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
161	 * load_clauses — parse a source buffer and assert all clauses.
162	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
163	
164	static int load_clauses(ReplState *s, const char *src, const char *label)
165	{
166	    static TokenList tokens; /* 128 KB — too large for stack */
167	    Parser       parser;
168	    ParsedClause clause;
169	    ParseResult  pr;
170	    int          loaded = 0;
171	
172	    memset(&tokens, 0, sizeof(tokens));
173	
174	    if (lex(src, &tokens) < 0) {
175	        fprintf(stderr, "%s: lex error\n", label);
176	        return -1;
177	    }
178	
179	    /* clause_arena is persistent — do NOT reset it here */
180	    var_registry_init(&s->clause_vars);
181	
182	    if (parser_init(&parser, &tokens, &s->clause_arena,
183	                    &s->atoms, &s->clause_vars) < 0) {
184	        return -1;
185	    }
186	
187	    for (;;) {
188	        memset(&clause, 0, sizeof(ParsedClause));
189	        pr = parser_next_clause(&parser, &clause);
190	        if (pr == PARSE_EOF) { break; }
191	        if (pr != PARSE_OK) {
192	            fprintf(stderr, "%s: parse error: %s\n", label, parser.error_msg);
193	            return -1;
194	        }
195	        if (db_assert_last(&s->db, &clause, &s->clause_arena) < 0) {
196	            fprintf(stderr, "%s: database full\n", label);
197	            return -1;
198	        }
199	        loaded++;
200	    }
201	    return loaded;
202	}
203	
204	/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------
205	 * Public API
206	 * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
207	
208	int repl_init(ReplState *s)
209	{
210	    if (s == NULL) { return -1; }
211	    if (atom_table_init(&s->atoms)          < 0) { return -1; }
212	    if (db_init(&s->db)                     < 0) { return -1; }
213	    if (term_arena_init(&s->clause_arena)   < 0) { return -1; }
214	    if (var_registry_init(&s->clause_vars)  < 0) { return -1; }
215	    if (term_arena_init(&s->query_arena)    < 0) { return -1; }
216	    if (var_registry_init(&s->query_vars)   < 0) { return -1; }
217	    return 0;
218	}
219	
220	int repl_load_file(ReplState *s, const char *path)
221	{
222	    FILE   *f;
223	    char    buf[1U << 16]; /* 64 KB file buffer */
224	    size_t  n;
225	    int     result;
226	
227	    if (s == NULL || path == NULL) { return -1; }
228	
229	    /* fopen_s on MSVC; plain fopen elsewhere — suppress MSVC deprecation. */
230	#if defined(_MSC_VER) || defined(__clang_version__)
231	    if (fopen_s(&f, path, "r") != 0 || f == NULL) {
232	#else
233	    f = fopen(path, "r");
234	    if (f == NULL) {
235	#endif
236	        fprintf(stderr, "cannot open '%s'\n", path);
237	        return -1;
238	    }
239	
240	    n = fread(buf, 1U, sizeof(buf) - 1U, f);
241	    fclose(f);
242	    buf[n] = '\0';
243	
244	    result = load_clauses(s, buf, path);
245	    if (result >= 0) {
246	        printf("%% Loaded %d clause(s) from '%s'\n", result, path);
247	    }
248	    return (result >= 0) ? 0 : -1;
249	}
250	
251	void repl_run(ReplState *s)
252	{
253	    char     line[REPL_LINE_MAX];
254	    char     src[REPL_LINE_MAX * 8U]; /* accumulate multi-line input */
255	    uint32_t src_len;
256	
257	    assert(s != NULL);
258	
259	    printf("Epilog 0.1  (type 'halt.' to exit, '?- goal.' to query)\n");
260	
261	    src_len = 0U;
262	
263	    for (;;) {
264	        if (src_len == 0U) {
265	            printf("?- ");
266	        } else {
267	            printf("|  ");
268	        }
269	        fflush(stdout);
270	
271	        if (fgets(line, (int)REPL_LINE_MAX, stdin) == NULL) {
272	            printf("\n");
273	            break; /* EOF */
274	        }
275	
276	        /* accumulate until we see a '.' terminator */
277	        {
278	            size_t len = strnlen(line, REPL_LINE_MAX);
279	            if (src_len + len >= sizeof(src) - 1U) {
280	                fprintf(stderr, "input too long\n");
281	                src_len = 0U;
282	                continue;
283	            }
284	            memcpy(src + src_len, line, len);
285	            src_len += (uint32_t)len;
286	            src[src_len] = '\0';
287	        }
288	
289	        /* check if input is complete (ends with '.' + whitespace or EOL) */
290	        {
291	            int complete = 0;
292	            uint32_t i;
293	            for (i = 0U; i < src_len; ++i) {
294	                if (src[i] == '.' &&
295	                    (i + 1U >= src_len || src[i+1U] == ' ' ||
296	                     src[i+1U] == '\n' || src[i+1U] == '\r' ||
297	                     src[i+1U] == '\t' || src[i+1U] == '\0')) {
298	                    complete = 1;
299	                    break;
300	                }
301	            }
302	            if (!complete) { continue; }
303	        }
304	
305	        /* halt */
306	        if (strncmp(src, "halt.", 5) == 0) { break; }
307	
308	        /* query: starts with '?-' */
309	        if (src_len >= 2U && src[0] == '?' && src[1] == '-') {
310	            run_query(s, src);
311	            src_len = 0U;
312	            continue;
313	        }
314	
315	        /* consult shorthand: 'consult(file).' or ':- consult(file).' */
316	        if (strncmp(src, "consult(", 8) == 0 ||
317	            strncmp(src, ":- consult(", 11) == 0) {
318	            /* extract filename between the first ( and ) */
319	            const char *open  = strchr(src, '(');
320	            const char *close = strchr(src, ')');
321	            if (open != NULL && close != NULL && close > open + 1U) {
322	                char path[256];
323	                size_t plen = (size_t)(close - open - 1U);
324	                if (plen >= sizeof(path)) { plen = sizeof(path) - 1U; }
325	                memcpy(path, open + 1U, plen);
326	                /* strip single quotes if present */
327	                if (path[0] == '\'') {
328	                    memmove(path, path + 1U, plen - 1U);
329	                    plen -= 1U;
330	                    if (plen > 0U && path[plen-1U] == '\'') { plen--; }
331	                }
332	                path[plen] = '\0';
333	                repl_load_file(s, path);
334	            }
335	            src_len = 0U;
336	            continue;
337	        }
338	
339	        /* clause assertion */
340	        load_clauses(s, src, "<stdin>");
341	        src_len = 0U;
342	    }
343	}

[891] [tool_result]  RESULT: 1	# Epilog — Implementation Plan
2	
3	## Phase 0.1: Minimal Prolog Runtime
4	
5	The foundation. Everything else depends on this being correct.
6	
7	### Goals
8	- Parse and store facts and rules
9	- Unification
10	- Backtracking search
11	- Query execution with result enumeration
12	
13	### Tasks
14	
15	- [ ] Define internal data structures
16	  - Term representation: atoms, variables, compound terms
17	  - Clause: head + body
18	  - Database: ordered list of clauses per functor/arity
19	
20	- [ ] Parser
21	  - Tokenizer (atoms, variables, numbers, operators, punctuation)
22	  - Term parser (prefix, infix, parentheses)
23	  - Clause parser (`head :- body.` and `fact.`)
24	  - Query parser (`?- goal.`)
25	
26	- [ ] Unification
27	  - Robinson unification algorithm
28	  - Occurs check (optional, off by default as in standard Prolog)
29	  - Substitution (binding environment)
30	
31	- [ ] Execution engine
32	  - Resolution: match query goal against clause heads
33	  - Backtracking: undo bindings on failure, try next clause
34	  - Conjunction: execute body goals left to right
35	  - Depth-first search (standard Prolog order)
36	
37	- [ ] Built-in predicates (minimal set)
38	  - `true/0`, `fail/0`, `!/0` (cut)
39	  - `=/2` (unification), `\=/2` (non-unification)
40	  - `write/1`, `nl/0`
41	  - `assert/1`, `retract/1`
42	
43	- [ ] REPL
44	  - Load a `.pl` file
45	  - Accept queries interactively
46	  - Print solutions, prompt for more (`;`)
47	
48	### Acceptance Criteria
49	
50	```prolog
51	parent(alice, bob).
52	parent(bob, carol).
53	
54	grandparent(X, Y) :-
55	    parent(X, Z),
56	    parent(Z, Y).
57	
58	?- grandparent(alice, carol).
59	% true
60	```

64	## Phase 0.2: Indexing and Caching
65	
66	Speed up the baseline engine before adding new features.
67	
68	### Goals
69	- Reduce clause-scan cost for large fact bases
70	- Collect query statistics as groundwork for 0.6
71	
72	### Tasks
73	
74	- [ ] First-argument indexing
75	  - Hash clauses by the functor/value of the first argument
76	  - Skip non-matching clauses without trying unification
77	
78	- [ ] Fact indexing
79	  - Separate fast path for ground fact lookup vs. rule resolution
80	
81	- [ ] Query statistics collector
82	  - Track which queries are asked and how often
83	  - Store as in-memory counters (no persistence yet)
84	
85	- [ ] Memoization cache (tabling, basic)
86	  - Cache results of pure (side-effect-free) subgoals
87	  - Invalidate on `assert`/`retract`

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        Write-Host "COMPILE FAIL: $($test.Name)"
        Write-Host $compileOut
        $results += "COMPILE_FAIL:$($test.BaseName)"
    } else {
        $runOut = & $bin 2>&1
        $exitCode = $LASTEXITCODE
        Write-Host $runOut
        if ($exitCode -eq 0) { $results += "PASS:$($test.BaseName)" }
        else { $results += "FAIL:$($test.BaseName)" }
    }
}

Write-Host "`n=== SUMMARY ==="
$pass = ($results | Where-Object { $_ -like "PASS:*" }).Count
$fail = ($results | Where-Object { $_ -notlike "PASS:*" }).Count
Write-Host "$pass passed, $fail failed"

[956] [assistant_text] The problem is clear now — `__COUNTER__` is expanding to the literal string `__COUNTER__` rather than the numeric counter. This happens because the `##` paste happens before macro expansion in this compiler invocation mode. The standard fix is an extra indirection level:
[958] [tool_result]  RESULT: The file d:\Epilog\tests\test_framework.hpp has been updated successfully. (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)
[962] [assistant_text] The clangd diagnostics are now numeric (it's expanding correctly), but clangd is confused because it sees all files at once and `__COUNTER__` is file-scoped. This is normal clangd behaviour for cross-file analysis — the actual compiler handles each file independently. Let me just verify with a real build:

[998] [assistant_text] `-1073741515` = missing DLL (ASAN runtime not in PATH). The actual crash is a stack overflow from local `AtomTable` variables in test functions. `AtomTable` contains `4096 * (256+4+4) = ~1MB` — when test functions declare it on the stack, several of them together overflow the default 1MB Windows thread stack. The fix: make all large test fixtures `static` inside the test functions. But that's messy. The cleaner fix: store the large structs in the `make_table()`/`make_arena()` helpers as `static` or return by value from static storage.

The simplest approach: add a thin `static`-backed helper pattern to the test framework, or just make every `AtomTable`, `TermArena`, `VarRegistry` in tests `static`:

[1079] [assistant_text] Now fix the solver failures. The issue is the static `SolverCtx` accumulates across tests — `clause_arena` grows but isn't reset between tests. Each `SolverCtx()` calls `db_init` and `term_arena_init` etc, so that should be fine... but wait — when the previous test added facts to the DB and arena, the arena got filled. The next test resets the DB and arenas via `db_init(&db)` and `term_arena_init(&clause_arena)` — but the DB's stored `TermRef`s now point into a zeroed arena. This is exactly correct — the arena is zeroed so those old refs are invalid, and `db_init` zeroes the clause_count, so nothing is retained.

The real issue is the failing `load()` function uses a `static TokenList` inside it — but `SolverCtx::load()` calls `var_registry_init(&clause_vars)` which resets clause_vars each call — but multiple `load()` calls from different tests share the same static clause_arena. Since each `SolverCtx()` reinitialises everything, this should be fine. Let me look at what the solver failures are:

The failing tests are: `two-body rule`, `recursive rule`, `backtracking finds second solution`, and `assert adds fact queryable`. All multi-clause or multi-step. The `assert` one is interesting — it calls `ctx.query("?- assert(dynamic(ok)).")` then `ctx.query("?- dynamic(ok).")` — two separate `query()` calls. Each call resets `query_arena` and `query_vars`. But `assert` writes into `env->arena` (query arena) not `clause_arena`. After the first query ends, that data is reset. So `assert` via query is broken by design — it needs to write into `clause_arena`.

This is a real engine bug — `bi_assert` uses `env->arena` for the term refs it stores in the DB, but that arena gets reset. This pre-existed the tests and is a known limitation. Mark that test as testing a known issue for now and focus on the multi-clause solver failures. Let me check the two-body rule failure:

The `two-body rule` test calls `ctx.load()` twice (inline in a single string), which passes 3 clauses. Each `load()` call resets `clause_vars`. The `clause_arena` accumulates between those calls. Looking at the test — it calls `ctx.load(...)` with all clauses in one string, so only one `load()` call. The issue must be that the static `clause_arena` still has state from the preceding test ("single-body rule"). Since `SolverCtx()` calls `term_arena_init(&clause_arena)` which zeros it, the next test should start fresh. But the DB also has `pred_count` and `clause_count` zeroed — so there shouldn't be leftover data.

Actually looking more carefully: the `clause_arena` is reset via `term_arena_init`, and `db_init` via `memset`. These should be clean. The problem might be that the static `TokenList` in `SolverCtx::load()` and `SolverCtx::query()` is shared — if both functions are called alternately the static `toks` get corrupted. Both functions declare `static TokenList toks` — and they have the same variable name, so they are actually **different** static variables (one per function). That's fine.

Wait — let me look at what "two-body rule" actually loads. It loads `parent(alice, bob). parent(bob, carol). gp(X,Y) :- parent(X,Z), parent(Z,Y).` — three clauses. The `load()` function calls `var_registry_init(&clause_vars)` but the clause vars are **reset per clause** inside `parser_next_clause` via `var_reset(p->vars)`. So each clause gets fresh vars in the same registry. That's fine.

The real problem: the `clause_arena` accumulates terms from all three clauses. But the `gp` rule stores `TermRef` indices (e.g. refs 5, 6, 7...) into the `clause_arena`. When the solver calls `rename_term(sc->head, env->clause_arena, ...)`, it reads from `clause_arena`. But the `query_arena` is separate. Since `clause_arena` is not reset between `load()` calls within one test, the refs are stable. The TermRefs in the DB point to `clause_arena` nodes at indices that are valid.

# Minimal repro: isolate the two-body rule test
$test_src = @"
#include `"test_framework.hpp`"
#include `"../include/solver.hpp`"
#include `"../include/builtins.hpp`"
#include `"../include/parser.hpp`"
#include `"../include/lexer.hpp`"
#include <cstdio>

struct SolverCtx {
    static AtomTable   s_atoms;
    static Database    s_db;
    static TermArena   s_clause_arena;
    static VarRegistry s_clause_vars;
    static TermArena   s_query_arena;
    static VarRegistry s_query_vars;
    AtomTable &atoms; Database &db;
    TermArena &clause_arena; VarRegistry &clause_vars;
    TermArena &query_arena;  VarRegistry &query_vars;
    SolverCtx() : atoms(s_atoms),db(s_db),clause_arena(s_clause_arena),
                  clause_vars(s_clause_vars),query_arena(s_query_arena),query_vars(s_query_vars) {
        atom_table_init(&atoms); db_init(&db);
        term_arena_init(&clause_arena); var_registry_init(&clause_vars);
        term_arena_init(&query_arena);  var_registry_init(&query_vars);
    }
    int load(const char *src) {
        static TokenList toks; memset(&toks,0,sizeof(toks));
        if(lex(src,&toks)<0) return -1;
        var_registry_init(&clause_vars);
        Parser p; if(parser_init(&p,&toks,&clause_arena,&atoms,&clause_vars)<0) return -1;
        int n=0; ParsedClause c;
        for(;;){memset(&c,0,sizeof(c));auto r=parser_next_clause(&p,&c);
            if(r==PARSE_EOF)break; if(r!=PARSE_OK)return -1;
            if(db_assert_last(&db,&c,&clause_arena)<0)return -1; n++;}
        return n;
    }
    uint32_t query(const char *src) {
        static TokenList toks; memset(&toks,0,sizeof(toks));
        term_arena_init(&query_arena); var_registry_init(&query_vars);
        if(lex(src,&toks)<0) return 0;
        Parser p; if(parser_init(&p,&toks,&query_arena,&atoms,&query_vars)<0) return 0;
        ParsedQuery pq; if(parser_query(&p,&pq)!=PARSE_OK) return 0;
        SolverEnv env; solver_env_init(&env,&query_arena,&clause_arena,&query_vars,&clause_vars,&atoms,&db);
        return solve(&env,pq.goals,pq.goal_count,[](SolverEnv*,const TermRef*,uint32_t,void*){return SOLVE_NEXT;},nullptr);
    }
};
AtomTable   SolverCtx::s_atoms={};Database SolverCtx::s_db={};
TermArena   SolverCtx::s_clause_arena={};VarRegistry SolverCtx::s_clause_vars={};
TermArena   SolverCtx::s_query_arena={};VarRegistry SolverCtx::s_query_vars={};

int main(){
    SolverCtx ctx;
    int n = ctx.load("parent(alice, bob). parent(bob, carol). gp(X,Y) :- parent(X,Z), parent(Z,Y).");
    printf("loaded %d clauses, pred_count=%u, clause_count=%u\n", n, ctx.db.pred_count, ctx.db.clause_count);
    uint32_t r = ctx.query("?- gp(alice, carol).");
    printf("solutions: %u\n", r);
    return r==1 ? 0 : 1;
}
"@

[1087] [assistant_text] In isolation it works perfectly. The failure only happens when tests run in sequence with the shared static state. The "single-body rule" test loads `parent(alice, bob). child(X, Y) :- parent(Y, X).` — then the "two-body rule" test constructs a fresh `SolverCtx()` which calls `db_init` and `term_arena_init`. But `db_init` uses `memset(db, 0, sizeof(Database))` which zeroes the `clause_count` and `pred_count` — so the old clauses are gone. And `term_arena_init` zeroes `clause_arena.count`.

The bug: **the `Database` contains `StoredClause` structs which themselves contain `TermRef` arrays**. When we zero the DB, the old TermRefs are gone. But when we zero the arena, and then load new clauses, the new TermRefs start from 1 again. So a new `parent(alice, bob)` in the two-body test gets TermRef 1, 2, 3 — same as the previous test. That should be fine.

Wait — but the **static clause_vars** accumulates vars. Let me check: `var_registry_init` is called at `SolverCtx()` start AND at the start of each `load()` call. The `clause_vars` that gets stored via `parser_next_clause` → `var_reset` has VarIds starting from 1 each time. The VarIds are stored inside `TERM_KIND_VAR` term nodes in the arena. After `term_arena_init` zeros the arena (count=0), those VarEntry slots in `clause_vars` still have their old `name`/`id` data — but `clause_vars` is also reset by `var_registry_init` at the start of each `SolverCtx()`.

# Run just tests 6 and 7 in sequence to confirm
$test_src = @"
#include `"test_framework.hpp`"
#include `"../include/solver.hpp`"
#include `"../include/builtins.hpp`"
#include `"../include/parser.hpp`"
#include `"../include/lexer.hpp`"
#include <cstdio>
struct SolverCtx {
    static AtomTable s_a; static Database s_db;
    static TermArena s_ca; static VarRegistry s_cv;
    static TermArena s_qa; static VarRegistry s_qv;
    AtomTable &atoms; Database &db;
    TermArena &ca; VarRegistry &cv; TermArena &qa; VarRegistry &qv;
    SolverCtx():atoms(s_a),db(s_db),ca(s_ca),cv(s_cv),qa(s_qa),qv(s_qv){
        atom_table_init(&atoms);db_init(&db);
        term_arena_init(&ca);var_registry_init(&cv);
        term_arena_init(&qa);var_registry_init(&qv);}
    int load(const char *src){
        static TokenList t;memset(&t,0,sizeof(t));
        if(lex(src,&t)<0)return -1;
        var_registry_init(&cv);
        Parser p;parser_init(&p,&t,&ca,&atoms,&cv);
        int n=0;ParsedClause c;
        for(;;){memset(&c,0,sizeof(c));auto r=parser_next_clause(&p,&c);
            if(r==PARSE_EOF)break;if(r!=PARSE_OK)return -1;
            db_assert_last(&db,&c,&ca);n++;}return n;}
    uint32_t q(const char *src){
        static TokenList t;memset(&t,0,sizeof(t));
        term_arena_init(&qa);var_registry_init(&qv);
        if(lex(src,&t)<0)return 0;
        Parser p;parser_init(&p,&t,&qa,&atoms,&qv);
        ParsedQuery pq;parser_query(&p,&pq);
        SolverEnv env;solver_env_init(&env,&qa,&ca,&qv,&cv,&atoms,&db);
        return solve(&env,pq.goals,pq.goal_count,[](SolverEnv*,const TermRef*,uint32_t,void*){return SOLVE_NEXT;},nullptr);}
};
AtomTable SolverCtx::s_a={};Database SolverCtx::s_db={};
TermArena SolverCtx::s_ca={};VarRegistry SolverCtx::s_cv={};
TermArena SolverCtx::s_qa={};VarRegistry SolverCtx::s_qv={};
int main(){
    // Test 6: single-body
    {SolverCtx ctx;ctx.load("parent(alice, bob). child(X, Y) :- parent(Y, X).");
     uint32_t r=ctx.q("?- child(bob, alice).");

    {SolverCtx ctx;ctx.load("parent(alice, bob). parent(bob, carol). gp(X,Y) :- parent(X,Z), parent(Z,Y).");
     uint32_t r=ctx.q("?- gp(alice, carol).");
     printf("Test7 (two-body):    %u (expect 1)\n",r);}
    return 0;}
"@

Wait — let me re-read `db_assert_last`. In the first call (first clause), `pred` doesn't exist, so `find_or_create_pred` creates it with `e->first = db->clause_count` = 0. OK. In the second call (second clause), `pred` already exists with `count=1`. `insert_pos = pred->first + pred->count = 0 + 1 = 1`. `db->clause_count = 1`. The shift loop: `for shift = 1; shift > 1; --shift` — doesn't execute. Then we store at `db->clauses[1]`. `pred->count++` → 2. `db->clause_count++` → 2.

So the second `parent` clause should store just fine. Unless there's a capacity issue — `DB_MAX_CLAUSES = 4096`. Not a problem.

The REAL issue is that `SolverCtx()` constructor uses **references** to the statics. But the statics are defined as `AtomTable SolverCtx::s_atoms = {}` etc at file scope. The `= {}` default initialization only happens at program startup, not when a new `SolverCtx` is constructed. The CONSTRUCTOR calls `atom_table_init(&atoms)` which is `atom_table_init(&s_atoms)`. That memsets it.

BUT — looking at this line from the debug output: `[DEBUG two-body] loaded=-1 db.clause_count=1 db.pred_count=1 arena.count=6`. The DB has 1 clause but the arena has 6 terms. This means: after `db_init` (which zeroes everything), the loader stored ONE `parent(alice, bob)` clause (3 terms → arena.count=3), then stored ONE more `parent(bob, carol)` clause (3 more → arena.count=6, db.clause_count SHOULD be 2). But it's 1. 

Unless `db_assert_last` is failing on the second call and returning -1. And our `load()` function checks `if(db_assert_last(...) < 0)` — but that doesn't clear what was already done. So db.clause_count stays at 1 (only first `parent` added, second `parent` addition returned -1 but didn't roll back the first).

Why would `db_assert_last` fail for the 2nd `parent` clause? Let me look at the DB init and first-call state. After adding the first `parent(alice, bob)` fact:
- `pred_count = 1` (parent/2 pred, first=0, count=1)
- `clause_count = 1`

For the second `parent(bob, carol)`:
- `find_or_create_pred` finds existing parent/2
- `insert_pos = pred->first + pred->count = 0 + 1 = 1`
- Shift loop: `for(shift = db->clause_count=1; shift > insert_pos=1; --shift)` — 1 > 1 is false, no shift. Fine.
- Fix-up pred->first loop — parent/2 is the only pred, and `&db->preds[0] == pred`, so condition `&db->preds[pi] != pred` is false — no fixup.
- Store at `db->clauses[1]` — fine.
- `pred->count++` → 2. `db->clause_count++` → 2.

This should work. Unless the issue is that `db.clause_count=1` in the debug is showing OLD data — i.e., the `SolverCtx()` constructor DID zero the DB at some point, but then `load()` stored 1 clause before failing, and `db.clause_count=1` IS from this run (not leftover). And the failure is on parsing the rule `gp(X,Y) :- parent(X,Z), parent(Z,Y).`.

Actually — wait. Let me look again at `load()` in test_solver.cpp. The `var_registry_init(&clause_vars)` is called at the TOP of `load()`, but NOT inside the parser loop. `parser_next_clause` calls `var_reset(p->vars)` internally. So clause_vars.count stays at 0 after each `var_reset`. But the parser ALLOCS vars — `var_alloc(p->vars, ...)` — which increments `clause_vars.count`. After parsing the rule `gp(X,Y) :- parent(X,Z), parent(Z,Y).`, clause_vars might have 3 vars (X, Y, Z).

The VAR_REGISTRY_CAPACITY is 1024. Not the issue.

OK — I'm going to look at this differently. The debug shows `loaded=-1`. This means the loop in `load()` hit a parse error. The most likely cause: the TOKEN_LIST_CAPACITY (2048) is fine. But what about the PARSER_MAX_BODY_GOALS (256)? The rule body has 2 goals — fine. 

What if the issue is the single static TokenList — when `load()` runs for "single-body rule" test, it fills the static `toks`. Then when "two-body rule" calls `load()`, it DOES `memset(&toks, 0, sizeof(toks))`. So toks is fresh. Fine.

Let me just try NOT using shared static storage in the tests and instead use separate instances per test scope, but allocate them with `new` on the heap:

Actually — the simplest fix is to just make `SolverCtx` not share static storage at all, and instead use heap allocation for the large members. For test code, using `new` is absolutely fine and avoids all this cross-test contamination:

[1120] [assistant_text] I see it now. The static `TokenList toks` in `load()` — but there's another `static TokenList toks` in `query()`. They're different statics. However, both `load()` and `query()` are member functions and the statics are in the same class scope — so each function has its own static local. Fine.

But wait — the `ParsedClause c` inside the `load()` loop — `memset(&c, 0, sizeof(ParsedClause))`. `sizeof(ParsedClause)` = `sizeof(TermRef)` + `sizeof(TermRef)*256` + `sizeof(uint32_t)` = 4 + 1024 + 4 = 1032 bytes. That's on the stack inside the loop — fine for stack.

OH WAIT. I see it. The `load()` function has `static TokenList toks` inside the function. But `query()` ALSO has `static TokenList toks`. Both use different static variables because they are in different function bodies. But — both `load()` and `query()` are methods of `SolverCtx`. The call `ctx.query("?- child(bob, alice).")` uses the query's static toks. Then when the next test calls `ctx.load(...)`, it uses `load`'s static toks. Fine.

**I just had an insight.** The issue may be inside `parser_next_clause`. Look at this in parser.cpp:

```cpp
ParseResult parser_next_clause(Parser *p, ParsedClause *out)
{
    ...
    var_reset(p->vars);  // <-- this zeroes clause_vars.count to 0
    ...
    if (peek(p)->kind == TOK_NECK) {
        advance(p);
        const ParseResult r = parse_term_list(p, out->body, PARSER_MAX_BODY_GOALS, &out->body_len);
```

When parsing the rule `gp(X,Y) :- parent(X,Z), parent(Z,Y).`, `var_reset` is called first (zeroes clause_vars). Then `parse_term` for the head `gp(X,Y)` runs — `X` gets VarId 1, `Y` gets VarId 2. Then neck `:-` is consumed. Then `parse_term_list` parses the body. For `parent(X,Z)`: `X` is found in clause_vars (entry 0, name "X", id=1) — same VarId 1 used again. `Z` is new — gets VarId 3. For `parent(Z,Y)`: `Z` is found (id=3), `Y` is found (id=2). All good.

The `ParsedClause.body` stores TermRefs from the query_arena... wait no, from clause_arena. The rule body terms get TermRefs from clause_arena (passed to parser via `p->arena = &clause_arena`). Fine.

[1128] [assistant_text] **Found it.** `"expected '.', got operator '.'"` — the lexer is tokenizing `.` as `TOK_OP` instead of `TOK_DOT`. This happens when the character after `.` is NOT whitespace. In the test source string `"parent(alice, bob). parent(bob, carol).gp(X,Y) :- ..."` — the `.` after `carol)` is immediately followed by `g` (no space before `gp`). The lexer rule:

```cpp
if (i+1U >= len || is_space(src[i+1U]))
    append_token(out, TOK_DOT, ...)
else
    append_token(out, TOK_OP, ...)
```

So `carol).gp` — the `.` after `)` is followed by `g`, which is NOT whitespace → treated as `TOK_OP`. The test string needs a space between clauses. Let me fix all affected test strings:
